\{1\}

\mu_{2}

\{1\}

Now
\mathrm{X}^{\ast}(\Out_{\mathrm{X}}(\mathrm{H}))\simeq X^{\ast}(Z({\mathrm{G}}))[2]
. It is now a classical fact that
X^{\ast}(Z({\mathrm{G}}))=X^{\ast}(\mathrm{A})/\mathbb{Z}\Phi
is generated by the minuscule weights of
{\mathrm{G}}
.

Remark 6.18
.

In type
A_{2n+1}
, the distinguished fundamental representation is
\wedge^{n}V
, where
V
is the standard representation of
{\mathrm{G}}=\SL(V)
.

We now turn to the varieties
\Spin(V_{1})\times^{\mu_{2}}\Spin(V_{2})\backslash\Spin(V)
. When
\theta
is an inner involution (either
\dim(V)
odd, or
\dim(V)
even and
\dim(V)\equiv\dim(V_{1})\pmod{2}
),
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}(\overline{k})
has a unique non-trivial element acting via the (diagonal) outer automorphism on the factors of
\mathrm{H}
of type
D
. In both cases,
X^{\ast}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}

is generated by the image of the fundamental weight associated to the spin representation or the two half-spin representations, depending on the parity.

Finally, we note that the varieties
\mathrm{X}=\Spin_{2a+1}\times^{\mu_{2}}\Spin_{2(n-a)-1}\backslash\Spin_{2n}
for
n\neq 2a+1
satisfies that
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\flat}\simeq\mu_{2}
as the involution is outer. In particular,
\Out_{\mathrm{X}}(\mathrm{H})
is trivial.

In all cases, the groups calculated agree with the claim regarding the nilpotent cone by comparing with
[
Lev07
, Proposition 6.22]
.
∎

Remark 6.19
.

It is reasonable to ask if this relationship with the nilpotent cone is general for symmetric varieties. This holds when
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}=\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}
but fails for distinguished roots of type (
B
). For example, in the case of
\mathrm{X}=\Sp_{2n}\times\Sp_{2n}\backslash\Sp_{4n}
from Example
3.21
as well as
\mathrm{X}=\Spin_{2n}\backslash\Spin_{2n+1}
, where the nilpotent cone is irreducible.

6.3.2.
Distinguished roots in the absolutely simple case

We saw in the proof of Lemma
6.14
, that when
{\mathrm{G}}
is simply connected and absolutely simple,
\theta
a
k
-involution and
\mathrm{X}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
, then
|\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}|\leq 1
. The following lemma completely classifies those cases when
|\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}|=1
over
\overline{k}
.

Lemma 6.20
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is simply connected and absolutely simple,
\theta
a
k
-involution and
\mathrm{X}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
with
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}\neq\emptyset
. Then
\Delta^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}=\{\alpha\}
and there are two cases:

(1)

if
\alpha
is a double root, then
({\mathrm{G}},{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
is a
k
-form of one of the following pairs:

(a)

(\SL_{2n},S(\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}))
,
n\geq 1
. Here,
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{n}
;

(b)

(\Spin_{n},\Spin_{2}\times^{\mu_{2}}\Spin_{n-2})
,
n\geq 5
;
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{2}
;

(c)

(\Spin_{4n},\GL_{2n}),

n\geq 2
;
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{n}
;

(d)

(\Sp_{2n},\GL_{n}),

n\geq 2
;
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{n}
;

(e)

(E_{7},\mathbb{G}_{m}\cdot E_{6})
;
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{3}
.

(2)

If
\alpha
is of type
B
, then
({\mathrm{G}},{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
is a
k
-form of one of the following pairs:

(a)

(\mathrm{Spin}_{2n+1},\mathrm{Spin}_{2n})
for
n\geq 2
. Here,
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{1}
;

(b)

(\Sp_{4n},\Sp_{2n}\times\Sp_{2n})
for
n\geq 1
. Here,
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
has type
B_{n}
.

For all pairs, the normalized root system
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}^{sv}
is also of type
B
, except for the pairs
(\Spin_{2n+1},\Spin_{2}\times^{\mu_{2}}\Spin_{2n-1})
with
n\geq 2
and
(\Sp_{2n},\GL_{n}),

n\geq 2
, where
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}^{sv}
is of type
C
. These are the only cases of Chevalley type.

Proof.

The classification of simple systems with a doubled root is precisely the content of
[
Kno96
, Remark after Cor. 7.6]
, and is verified by direct calculation from the classification. For the classification of systems of type
B
roots, one may either consult the classification
[
Spr87
]
or Losev’s analysis of distinguished roots
[
Los09
, Section 4]
.
∎

6.4.
Outer data for well-adapted varieties

Let
{\mathrm{G}}
be a quasi-split reductive group over
k
with
k
-rational involution
\theta
. We fix a
k
-rational Borel pair
(\mathrm{A},\B)
which is
(\theta,k)
-admissible.

On the basis of Theorems
6.1
and
6.4
, we impose the following restriction.

Assumption 6.21
.

Assume that the symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}_{sc}
-variety
\mathrm{X}_{sc}=\mathrm{H}_{sc}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{sc}
contains no simple factors of Chevalley type and that Conjecture
3.17
holds for
\mathrm{X}
.

Under this assumption, we know that
\mathrm{X}
is well-adapted and up to replacing
\mathrm{X}
with a
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner twist, we may assume that
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
satisfies that
\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
is quasi-split over
k
. The current section is in service of Section
9
, so we will simplify and also assume for simplicity that
\mathrm{H}
is connected, leaving discussion of disconnected groups to future work.

Recall from Section
3.4
, there is a natural isomorphism

\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})=\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}\Res_{{k_{i}/k}}(\mu_{2}),

where
I_{\mathrm{X}}
is the set of
\Gamma
-orbits in
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
. Following the reductions in Section
6.1
, there thus exists a set of finite separable extensions
k_{i}/k

(i\in I_{\mathrm{X}})
and
k_{j}/k
(
j\in J_{\mathrm{X}}
) (unique up to unique isomorphism) with a decomposition
\mathrm{X}_{sc}=\prod_{i}\mathrm{X}_{i}\times\prod_{j}\mathrm{X}_{j}
where
\mathrm{X}_{i}=\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{i})
such that
\mathrm{X}_{i}^{\prime}={\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}_{i}/\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{i}
is a symmetric variety associated to an absolutely simple and simply connected
k_{i}
-group
{\mathrm{G}}_{i}^{\prime}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{i}^{\prime}}^{dist}=\{\gamma_{i}\}
, and where
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{j}}^{dist}=\emptyset
.

Fix
i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}
. Over the field extension
k_{i}/k
, the distinguished root
\gamma_{i}
induces the canonical quadratic character (cf.
[
BG22
, Proposition C.5]
)

(6.2)

\epsilon_{i}:=\mu_{\gamma_{i}}:\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{i}}\longrightarrow\mu_{2},

dual to the inclusion
\langle\gamma\rangle/\langle 2\gamma\rangle\subset X^{\ast}(\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}))\subset X^{\ast}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}})
.
Taking the
\Gamma
-action into account, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Res(\epsilon_{i})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{i}}

where
\Res(\epsilon_{i})
is obtained functorially from
\epsilon_{i}
via the standard adjunction
[
CGP10
, Eq. A.5.1]
and

p_{i}:\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})\longrightarrow\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\Aut_{d}({\mathrm{X}_{i}^{\prime}}))\overset{\Res(\epsilon_{i})}{\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2})

denotes the canonical projection dual to the inclusion
\Ind_{\Gamma_{i}}^{\Gamma}(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\gamma_{i})\subset X^{\ast}(\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})).

Consider now the geometric cocycle
\mu_{\mathrm{X}}:=\mu_{\mathrm{X}}^{d}:\Gamma\longrightarrow\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})(\overline{k})
5
5

5

We drop the superscript
d
for notational simplicity. Strictly speaking, this conflicts with the notation
\mu_{\mathrm{X}}:\Gamma\to\mathrm{S}\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}
, but we will not refer to this section again in the paper.
as in Section
3.4.3
. For each
i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}
, consider the composition

\mu_{\mathrm{X},i}:=p_{i}\circ\mu_{\mathrm{X}}:\Gamma\longrightarrow\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2})(\overline{k}).

Clearly
\mu_{\mathrm{X}}=\prod_{i}\mu_{\mathrm{X},i}
.

Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2})({\overline{k}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{2}(\overline{k}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\mathrm{X},i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
j
is the canonical unit map (e.g.
[
CGP10
, Proposition A.5.7]
) and where the cocycle
\mu_{i}
is determined in order that the diagram commutes. Shapiro’s lemma implies that the cohomology class
[\mu_{\mathrm{X},i}]\in H^{1}(k,\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2}))
is uniquely determined by the classes
[\mu_{i}]\in H^{1}(k_{i},\mu_{2})
. In particular,
\mu_{X}
is determined up to coboundary by
\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}
via the isomorphisms

(6.3)

\displaystyle H^{1}(k,\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}))

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}H^{1}(k,\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2}))

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}H^{1}(k_{i},\mu_{2})\simeq\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}\Hom(\Gamma_{i},\mu_{2}(\overline{k})).

We record this in the following lemma.

Lemma/Definition 6.22
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is quasi-split and
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is a symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety satisfying Assumption
6.21
and that
\mathrm{H}
is connected.
With respect to the isomorphism (
6.3
), the data

(6.4)

\mathrm{out}_{\mathrm{X}}:=\{\mu_{i}:\Gamma_{i}\longrightarrow\{\pm 1\}:i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}\}

uniquely determines the cohomology class
[\mu_{\mathrm{X}}]\in H^{1}(k,\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}))
. We refer to
\out_{\mathrm{X}}
as the
outer data
of
\mathrm{X}
.

In particular, if
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is another
{\mathrm{G}}
-form of
\mathrm{X}
, then
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
are
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner forms if and only if
\out_{\mathrm{X}}=\out_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Considering our discussion above, only the final claim needs justification. But this is a direct consequence of the well-adaptedness of
\mathrm{X}
(and hence of
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
) and Lemma
2.7
.
∎

Example 6.23
.

Returning to Example
3.7
, for both varieties
\mathrm{X}_{1}
and
\mathrm{X}_{t}
the unique spherical root is a double root. An easy calculation shows

\out_{\mathrm{X}_{1}}=\{\mu=1\},\qquad\out_{\mathrm{X}_{t}}=\{\mu=\eta_{E/k}:\Gamma\longrightarrow\{\pm 1\}\},

where
\eta_{E/k}
it the unique non-trivial quadratic character of
\Gamma
trivial on the normal subgroup
\Gal(\overline{k}/E)\subset\Gamma
.

Part II
The dual symmetric variety and endoscopic data

7.
The dual group and dual symmetric variety

We recall now the formalism of the dual group of a spherical variety and discuss corresponding notions of
L
-groups. In the case of symmetric varieties, we introduce the notion of the dual symmetric variety
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
and study its properties. The key statements are Lemma
7.14
and Proposition
7.16
.

7.1.
The dual group of a spherical variety

Let
{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected reductive group over
k
. Let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be a spherical
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety and let
\Omega_{\mathrm{X}}=({\mathfrak{X}},{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}},\Omega^{(1)},\Omega^{(2)})
be the homogeneous spherical datum determined by a choice of Borel pair
(\mathrm{A},\B)
. Passing to the normalized root system, Sakellaridis and Venkatesh show that
({\mathfrak{X}}^{sv},{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}},\check{\mathfrak{X}}^{sv},\check{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}})
is a based root datum.

Definition 7.1
.

The dual group of the
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety
\mathrm{X}
is the connected complex reductive group
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
associated to the dual based root datum
(\check{\mathfrak{X}}^{sv},\check{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}},{\mathfrak{X}}^{sv},{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}})
.

Now the surjection
\mathrm{A}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}
(or more explicitly, the inclusion
{\mathfrak{X}}\subset X^{\ast}(\T)
) produces a canonical morphism
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}
. Considering the inclusions of lattices

{\mathfrak{X}}\subset\mathfrak{X}^{sv}=\mathfrak{X}+\mathbb{Z}\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{sv}\subset X^{\ast}(\T).

we obtain a sequence

\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\overset{\nu_{\mathrm{A}}}{\longrightarrow}\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\overset{\varphi_{\mathrm{A}}}{\longrightarrow}\check{\mathrm{A}},

where
\nu_{\mathrm{A}}
is a finite morphism.

Equip the dual group
{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
of
{\mathrm{G}}
with a pinning
e_{\alpha^{\vee}}\in\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha^{\vee}}^{\vee}
for each
\alpha\in\Delta
. For each
\sigma\in{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
, Knop defines in
[
Kno19
]
a one-dimensional subspace
\mathfrak{g}_{\check{\sigma}}^{\vee}
of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
by

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\check{\sigma}}^{\vee}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{g}_{\check{\sigma}}^{\vee}&:\sigma\in R^{+},\\
[\mathfrak{g}_{\beta^{\vee}}^{\vee},e_{\delta_{1}^{\vee}}-e_{\delta_{2}^{\vee}}]&:\sigma\text{ is of type $D_{n\geq 3}$},\\
[\mathfrak{g}_{\beta^{\vee}}^{\vee},2e_{\delta_{1}^{\vee}}-e_{\delta_{2}^{\vee}}]&:\sigma\text{ is of type $B_{3}^{\prime\prime}$},\\
\mathbb{C}(e_{\delta_{1}^{\vee}}-e_{\delta_{2}^{\vee}})&:\sigma\text{ is of type $D_{2}$};\end{cases}

here
\beta^{\vee}:=\gamma_{1}^{\vee}-\delta_{1}^{\vee}=\gamma_{2}^{\vee}-\delta_{2}^{\vee}
when
\sigma
is of type
G
. We remark that a choice is made in the case of type
D_{2}
roots, following the convention of
[
Kno19
]
. See also
[
BZSV23
, Section 4.8]
for discussion.

Definition 7.2
.

A homomorphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
is
distinguished
if
\varphi_{X}|_{\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}}=\varphi_{A}
. and
\varphi(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{X},\check{\sigma}}^{\vee})=\mathfrak{g}_{\check{\sigma}}^{\vee}
as defined in (
7.1
).

Theorem 7.3
.

[
KS17
, Theorem 9.7]

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}
is a spherical
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety. Then distinguished morphisms
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
exist. Moreover, they are unique up to
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}
-conjugacy.
6
6

6

In fact, one needs to restrict to a certain subtorus in the presence of certain types of spherical root; see
[
KS17
]
for details.
The image

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}:=\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}({\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}_{\mathrm{X}})

is a well-defined subgroup of
{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
independent of
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Proof.

We recall a sketch of the proof, as its construction is important to us. Recall the set of
associated roots

\hat{\Sigma}
and coroots
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
. This construction gives rise
[
KS17
, Theorem 7.3]
to an additively-closed based root sub-datum

(X_{\ast}(\mathrm{A}),\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}},X^{\ast}(\mathrm{A}),\hat{\Sigma})\subset(X_{\ast}(\mathrm{A}),\check{\Phi},X^{\ast}(\mathrm{A}),\Phi)

Thus, there exists a unique reductive subgroup
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
corresponding to this subroot datum; this is called the associated group. The passage from spherical roots
\sigma\in{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
to the associated coroots
\{\check{\gamma}_{1},\check{\gamma}_{2}\}
corresponds to the notion of
folding
, and hence gives rise to a morphism

\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}

with the desired properties.
∎

Knop and Schalke show that the kernel of the homomorphism
\varphi_{X}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
agrees with the kernel of the map
\varphi_{\mathrm{A}}:\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}
. This isolates a useful class of spherical varieties, as named in
[
Sak21
]
.

Definition 7.4
.

We say that a spherical variety
\mathrm{X}
is
excellent
if it is affine, homogeneous, and the kernel of
\mathrm{A}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}
is connected.

We note that when
\mathrm{X}
is excellent, the cotangent bundle
T^{\ast}\mathrm{X}
is hyperspherical by Proposition 3.7.4 of
[
BZSV23
]
. This is stronger than having no spherical roots of type
N
, the simplest counterexample being
\mathrm{X}=\mathbb{G}_{m}\backslash\SL_{2}
.

We remark that Lemma
B.4
implies the following result.

Corollary 7.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is connected reductive group over
k
such that
{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
is simply connected. If
\mathrm{X}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is a symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety, it is excellent if and only if the pair
({\mathrm{G}},{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
is simply connected in the sense of
[
Les22
, Section 4]
.

In particular, the results of
loc. cit.
provide tools for the topological study of excellent symmetric varieties.

We also record the following useful property of the dual group.

Proposition 7.6
.

[
Kno19
, Theorem 1]

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{Y}
are spherical
{\mathrm{G}}
-varieties and assume that there exists a dominant morphism
\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}
. There exists a unique homomorphism with finite kernel
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{Y}}\to\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
which is compatible with the homomorphisms to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
.

Remark 7.7
.

In particular, if
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{H})
is a symmetric pair and
(\widetilde{\mathrm{G}},\widetilde{\mathrm{H}})
is a compatible
z
-extension, then

\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}={\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}}\backslash\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}=\mathrm{X}

is a dominant morphism of
\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}
-varieties. Noting that there is a canonical exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}}\longrightarrow\check{\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}}\longrightarrow\check{N}\longrightarrow 1,

one readily checks that diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}}}

commutes.

The set of parabolic roots
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{p}
corresponds to a Levi subgroup
\mathrm{A}\subset\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
and a dual Levi subgroup
\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
. Our choice of a pinning of
{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
induces one on
\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. In particular, the pinning determines a principal homomorphism
\iota_{\mathrm{X}}:\SL_{2}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. It was shown in
[
KS17
]
that the images of
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
and
\iota_{\mathrm{X}}
commute with each other in
{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
. We thus obtain a morphism

(7.2)

\xi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\times\SL_{2}\longrightarrow{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}.

The results of
[
Kno19
,
KS17
]
indicate how to extend this notion to incorporate the action of
\Gamma
on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Viewed as a subgroup of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
,
[
KS17
, Section 10]
shows that there is a unique
\Gamma
-action on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
such that the inclusion
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
intertwines with the
L
-action on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. We denote the corresponding semi-direct product by

{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\hookrightarrow{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}},

though this is arguably inappropriate as the
\Gamma
-action can fail to preserve the pinning on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
in some cases. The additional requirement that
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
preserve the embeddings (
7.1
) forces a
unique
action of
\Gamma
on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
with the property that the morphism
\xi_{\mathrm{X}}
intertwines the action with the above action on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. We thus tentatively define the Galois form of the
L
-group of
\mathrm{X}
to be

{}^{L}\mathrm{X}:=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\rtimes\Gamma

with respect to this unique action. The map
\xi_{\mathrm{X}}
thus extends uniquely to a morphism

(7.3)

{}^{L}\xi_{\mathrm{X}}:{}^{L}\mathrm{X}\times\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})\longrightarrow{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}}.

Remark 7.8
.

As noted above, Knop fixes the choice of image for
\mathfrak{g}_{\check{\sigma}}
for all
\sigma\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}
of type
D_{2}
in
[
Kno19
]
, which we adopt here. It is worth mentioning that other choices relate to replacing
\mathrm{H}
-periods with
(\mathrm{H},\chi)
-periods for certain characters
\chi
of
\mathrm{H}
; see
[
Pra20
]
and
[
BP18
]
.

7.2.
The dual symmetric variety

Note that
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}\sqcup\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}^{p}\subset\check{\Phi}^{+}
. There is a natural involution
{\vartheta}:\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
determined by the decomposition
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}}\hat{\alpha}
, where
\hat{\alpha}
denotes the set of associated coroots. Suppose now that
\mathrm{X}
is a symmetric variety associated with an involution
\theta
. The involution on the set
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
is compatible with an involution on
\hat{\mathrm{A}}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}
is a symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety associated to the involution
\theta
. Assume that
(\mathrm{A},\B)
are a
(\theta,k)
-admissible pair. Then the involution
{\vartheta}
of
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
agrees with the restriction of the involution

-\theta:X_{\ast}(\mathrm{A})\longrightarrow X_{\ast}(\mathrm{A}).

This follows from Lemma
B.5
.
Set
\check{\mathrm{A}}=X^{\ast}(\mathrm{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
for the complex dual torus. If
\check{\theta}:\check{\mathrm{A}}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}
denotes the involution induced by the action of
\theta
on
X^{\ast}(\mathrm{A})
, then
{-\theta}
induces the involution
{\vartheta}(t)=\theta(t)^{-1}
on
\check{\mathrm{A}}
.

Proposition 7.10
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is a symmetric variety associated to
\theta
and let
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
be a distinguished morphism. There exists an involution
\vartheta
of
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
extending the involution on
\check{\mathrm{A}}
such that

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}=\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})=(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\vartheta,\circ}.

It commutes with the given action of
\Gamma
on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. In particular, the quotient
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is a complex symmetric variety equipped with a
\Gamma
-action.

Remark 7.11
.

In the context of the affine Grassmannian for real groups, a similar result is discussed in
[
Nad05
, Section 10.7]
, with the translation that
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=L_{1}
.

Proof.

Corollary 7.9 of
[
KS17
]
implies the result on the level of adjoint groups. Indeed, the morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
is there constructed via the process of “folding” of the root system of
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, so that there exists an involution
\check{\theta}_{ad}
on the adjoint group
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}
.
We also know that
Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})=\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}^{-1}(Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}))
, so there exists a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}

inducing a surjective morphism

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X},ad}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}.

Their construction shows that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X},ad}=[\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}]^{\check{\theta}_{ad},\circ}
.

Steinberg’s theorem
[
Ste68
, 9.16]
allows us to lift this involution uniquely to the simply connected cover

\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},sc}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad};

call this involution
\check{\theta}_{sc}
. Recall that
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},sc}\times^{\hat{Z}_{sc}}Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})

[
Del79
, 2.0.1]
, where
\hat{Z}_{sc}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},sc}
is the center. Thus to show that there exists a lift of
\check{\theta}_{sc}
to
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, it suffices to show that the induced involutions on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{sc}
and
Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})\subset\check{\mathrm{A}}
agree on
\hat{Z}_{sc}
.

Recall that we fixed a
(\theta,k)
-admissible pair
(\mathrm{A},\B)
for
{\mathrm{G}}
.
Let
\vartheta
denote the involution on
\check{\mathrm{A}}
as in Lemma
7.9
.
We have the four groups

\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{sc}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{der}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{ad},

and the associated tori

\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{sc}\to\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{der}\subset\check{\mathrm{A}}\to\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{ad}.

Considering weight lattices, we obtain a
\Gamma
-equivariant commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker[\mathbb{Z}\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}\to X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}})]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coker[\mathbb{Z}\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}\to X^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{sc})]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

Lemma
7.9
implies that the involution
\vartheta
on
X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}})
is compatible with the given involution on
\mathbb{Z}\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
. We thus have a compatible involution on the cokernel, which is precisely the character group of
Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})
. Moreover, the compatibility between the involutions on
\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{sc}
and
\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{ad}
induce a compatible involution on
\coker[\mathbb{Z}\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}\to X^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{sc})],
which gives the character group of
\hat{Z}_{sc}
. Finally, the middle vertical arrow is equivariant with respect to these involutions. This shows that the involution on
Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})
is compatible with
\check{\theta}_{sc}
under the map
\iota:\hat{Z}_{sc}\to Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})
.
∎

Definition 7.12
.

Let
{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected reductive group over
k
and let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be a symmetric
k
-variety. Given a distinguished morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
, there exists a
dual involution
(unique up to
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\Gamma}
-conjugacy)

\vartheta:\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}

satisfying

(1)

\vartheta
commutes with the
\Gamma
-action on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
,

(2)

\vartheta|_{\check{\mathrm{A}}}=\mathrm{inv}\circ\check{\theta},
where
\mathrm{inv}:\check{\mathrm{A}}\to\check{\mathrm{A}}
denotes the inversion map,

(3)

\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}=\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\vartheta,\circ}
.

We define the
dual symmetric variety

\hat{\mathrm{X}}:=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
for
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
.

This variety sits in the diagram

(7.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{\mathrm{X}}}

where
\check{\tau}
is the quotient map and
\hat{s}_{\mathrm{X}}
is induced by the symmetrization map associated to the variety
(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\vartheta}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
into
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
.

7.3.
Minimality and split tori

Assume that
(\mathrm{A},\B)
is a
(\theta,k)
-admissible pair for
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{H})
and let
\vartheta:\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
denote the daul involution. Let
(\check{\mathrm{A}},\check{\B})
be the dual pair and denote by
\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}=\check{\B}\cap\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. By construction, the involution
\vartheta
preserves
\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}
, so that
\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
gives a
\vartheta
-stable Borel subgroup of
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Following Springer
[
S+85
]
, we say that the
\vartheta
-stable Borel pair
(\check{\mathrm{A}},\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}})
is a
fundamental pair
of
(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}},{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}})
.

Those symmetric varieties
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
which occur in this construction are strongly constrained. Indeed, Corollary 7.9 of
[
KS17
]
implies that the associated symmetric
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}
-variety
\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{ad}
satisfies the property of
minimality
, so that

\mathrm{rank}(\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{ad})=\mathrm{rank}(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},ad})-\mathrm{rank}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}/Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})).

This implies that
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
is also minimal
[
Res10
, Proposition 3.2]
. The following lemma recalls the most relevant properties of such spherical varieties.

Lemma 7.13
.

[
Bri04
, Lemma 4 and 5]

Suppose that
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{H},\theta)
is a symmetric pair over an algebraically closed field and set
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
. Then the following are equivalent

(1)

\mathrm{X}
is a minimal symmetric variety.

(2)

Any
\theta
-stable maximal torus of
{\mathrm{G}}
contains a maximal
\theta
-fixed subtorus (hence a maximal torus of
\mathrm{H}
).

(3)

Any
\theta
-stable maximal torus of
{\mathrm{G}}
contains a maximal
\theta
-split subtorus.

(4)

Any two
\theta
-stable maximal tori of
{\mathrm{G}}
are conjugate by
\mathrm{H}^{\circ}

Suppose
\mathrm{X}
is minimal. There exists a single
\mathrm{H}^{\circ}
-orbit of fundamental pairs
(\mathrm{A},\B)
.

In particular, every maximally
\vartheta
-split maximal torus is contained in a
\vartheta
-stable Borel subgroup and that such Borel subgroups form a single
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
-orbit.

Lemma 7.14
.

For any
k
-rational maximally
\theta
-split maximal torus
T\subset{\mathrm{G}}
, there exists an embedding
\check{T}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
such that the natural dual involution

\displaystyle\vartheta_{T}:\check{T}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\check{T}

induced by
-\theta
on
X^{\ast}(T)
intertwines with
\vartheta
. In particular, such an embedding realizes
\check{T}
as a maximally
\vartheta
-split torus. Any two such embeddings are conjugate by
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
, so there is a unique
\Gamma
-invariant
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
-orbit of such embeddings.

Proof.

Fix a maximally
\theta
-split Borel
B\supset T
. Since both
\mathrm{A}
and
T
are maximally
\theta
-split maximal tori, there exists
h\in({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}(\overline{k})\subset\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
such that
h\mathrm{A}h^{-1}=T
and
h\B h^{-1}=B
. This induces a
\theta
-equivariant isomorphism

X_{\ast}(\mathrm{A})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X_{\ast}(T)

carrying the root system
(\check{\Phi},\check{\Delta})
to one in
X_{\ast}(T)
. In particular, this induces a
\vartheta
-equivariant isomorphism

\check{j}:\check{T}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\check{\mathrm{A}}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}.

A standard fact is that the embedding
\check{j}
is unique up to
(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\Gamma}
-conjugacy
[
Kot82
]
, but not every conjugate will have a maximally
\vartheta
-split image. On the other hand. if two embeddings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{2}}

give two maximally
\vartheta
-split tori
j_{1}(\check{T}),j_{2}(\check{T})\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, they must be conjugate by an element of
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\vartheta,\circ}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}

[
Ric82
, pg.287]
.
∎

7.3.1.
Dual torus embedding

Conversely, we establish a dual embedding statement in Proposition
7.16
under an additional rationality assumption (Assumption
7.15
).

Assumption 7.15
.

Assume that
k
is perfect ant that for each regular semi-simple class
a\in[\mathrm{X}^{rss}\sslash\mathrm{H}](k)
, there exists some
\xi\in H^{1}(k,\mathrm{H})
with pure inner twist
(\mathrm{X}^{\xi},{\mathrm{G}}^{\xi})
and a regular semi-simple element
x_{a}\in X^{\xi}(k)
with invariant
a
.

The assumption may be stated as claiming that the map from the quotient stack
\mathrm{X}/\mathrm{H}
to the categorical quotient
\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}
is surjective on
k
-points when we restrict to the regular semi-simple locus. This is as good as can be hoped for in general. We verify this assumption in the case that the symmetric variety
\mathrm{X}
is quasi-split in
[
Les24
]
, and remark that certain general results which may obviate the need for this assumption in forthcoming work of Ngô–Morrissey
[
NM24
]
. Note that when
k
is finite (of good characteristic), then Assumption
7.15
holds. Indeed, every point
a\in[\mathrm{X}^{rss}\sslash\mathrm{H}](k)
corresponds to a
k
-rational semi-simple
({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}
-orbit, which possesses a
k
-point by Lang’s theorem.

Using this assumption, we wish to establish a converse of Lemma
7.14
. In preparation, suppose that we have
({\mathrm{G}},\theta)
as above and let
\mathrm{A}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
be a
k
-rational maximally
\theta
-split maximal torus of
{\mathrm{G}}
. Suppose we are given a
1
-cocycle
w:\Gamma\longrightarrow W(\overline{k})
valued in the Weyl group satisfying

(1)

w_{\sigma}(\mathrm{A}^{-})=\mathrm{A}^{-}
for all
\sigma\in\Gamma
, so that the cocyle is valued in
W_{1}=\{w\in W:w(\mathrm{A}^{-})=\mathrm{A}^{-}\}\subset W
,

(2)

\theta\circ w_{\sigma}=w_{\sigma}\circ\theta
for all
\sigma\in\Gamma
.

Let
{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}
denote the
k
-torus obtained by twisting
\mathrm{A}
by this cocycle. It possesses a
k
-rational involution
{}^{\ast}\theta:{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}\longrightarrow{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}
satisfying
{}^{\ast}(\mathrm{A}^{-})=({}^{\ast}\mathrm{A})^{-}
; we denote this
{}^{\ast}\theta
-split subtorus by
{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}^{-}
.

Recalling that
[
Ric82
, Section 4]

W_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq W(\mathrm{H},\mathrm{A}^{-})\simeq W_{1}/W_{2},

where and
W_{2}=\{w\in W_{1}:w|_{\mathrm{A}^{-}}\equiv Id\}
, we see that
w
descends to a
1
-cocycle valued in
W_{\mathrm{X}}
when we restrict to
\mathrm{A}^{-}
. Using the fact

W_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq N_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})/Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-}),

we see that for each
\sigma\in\Gamma
, the twisted Galois action
{}^{\ast}\sigma
may be realized as

{{}^{\ast}\sigma}(t)=n_{\sigma}{}^{\sigma}tn_{\sigma}^{-1}\qquad\text{for $t\in\mathrm{A}^{-}(\overline{k})$,}

where
n_{\sigma}\in N_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathrm{A})\cap({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}(\overline{k})
is an element representing
w_{\sigma}
. Note that
n_{\sigma}
is well defined up to right-multiplication by an element of
Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})
, but we may choose
n_{\sigma}
so that the cochain is continuous (cf.
[
Bor93
, Section 7]
).

Now assume
{\mathrm{G}}
is quasi-split and let
\mathrm{X}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be the associated symmetric variety with
\mathrm{H}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
. Assume Assumption
7.15
holds for
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
, and identify
\mathrm{X}\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}
via the symmetrization map. Set
x_{0}\in\mathrm{X}(k)
to be the base point.

Proposition 7.16
.

For any
\Gamma
-invariant
{\mathrm{G}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
-conjugacy class of maximally
\vartheta
-split maximal torus
\check{T}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, let
(T,\theta_{T})
denote the corresponding
k
-torus with involution. Setting
T^{-}
to the maximal
\theta_{T}
-split subtorus, there exists

(1)

a class
\xi\in H^{1}(k,\mathrm{H})
with pure inner twist
(\mathrm{X}^{\xi},{\mathrm{G}}^{\xi})

(2)

a
k
-rational embedding
{T}^{-}\to\mathrm{X}^{\xi}\subset{\mathrm{G}}^{\xi}
realizing
{T}^{-}
as a maximally
{\theta}^{\xi}
-split torus.

Remark 7.17
.

The embedding
T^{-}\to\mathrm{X}^{\xi}
identifies the image with the
flat

T^{-}\cdot x_{0}
through
x_{0}

[
Kno95
, Section 6]
.

Proof.

Set
\mathrm{H}=({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}
. Fix a
k
-rational maximally
\theta
-split maximal torus
\mathrm{A}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
. Since both
\check{\mathrm{A}}
and
\check{T}
are
\vartheta
-stable maximal tori, there exists
h\in\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
such that
\Ad(h)(\check{\mathrm{A}})=\check{T}
inducing a
\vartheta
-equivariant isomorphism

X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X_{\ast}(\check{T})

carrying the root system
(\Phi,\Delta)
to one in
X_{\ast}(\check{T})
in a
\vartheta
-equivariant way; this forces the Galois actions differ by a
1
-cocycle valued in the little Weyl group
W_{\mathrm{X}}
, realized as the Weyl group of
(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}},\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})
. This isomorphism induces a
{\theta}
-equivariant
\overline{k}
-isomorphism

{j}:{T}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{A}}\subset{\mathrm{G}}.

Thus, we obtain a
1
-cocycle
w:\Gamma\longrightarrow W(\overline{k})
satisfying (
1
) and (
2
). In particular, if we consider

j_{-}:T^{-}\to\mathrm{A}^{-},

we see that
{}^{\sigma}j_{-}:=\sigma\circ j_{-}\circ\sigma_{T}^{-1}=\Ad(n_{\sigma}^{-1})\circ j_{-}
for where
n_{\sigma}\in N_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})
is an element representing
w_{\sigma}
.

Let
{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}^{-}\subset{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}
be the twisted form of
\mathrm{A}
as above. If
k
is finite, consider the twisted form
{}^{\ast}[\mathrm{H}/Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})]
of
\mathrm{H}/Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})
by the cocycle
w
. That is, for
t\in[\mathrm{H}/Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})](k)
,
\sigma\in\Gamma
acts by
{}^{\sigma}tn_{\sigma}^{-1}
. By Lang’s theorem, there exists
t\in{}^{\ast}[\mathrm{H}/Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})](k)
. Thus, writing
t=hZ_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})
, we see that for all
\sigma\in\Gamma

hZ_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})={}^{\sigma}hn_{\sigma}^{-1}Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k}).

An easy calculation now show that
\Ad(h)\circ j:T^{-}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}
gives a
k
-rational embedding.

We now assume that
k
is infinite. In this case, it can happen that
{}^{\ast}[\mathrm{H}/Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})](k)=\emptyset
, so we proceed by applying Assumption
7.15
. By the assumptions on
k

[
Bor91
, 18.3]
,
{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}^{-}(k)
is Zariski-dense in
{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}^{-}
, so that there exists
x\in{}^{\ast}\mathrm{A}^{-}(k)
which gives a regular semi-simple element of
\mathrm{X}(\overline{k})\subset{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{k})
. By assumption,

x=n_{\sigma}{}^{\sigma}xn_{\sigma}^{-1},\qquad\text{where}\quad n_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{H}(\overline{k}).

This implies that the
\mathrm{H}
-orbit of
a\in\mathrm{X}(\overline{k})
is
k
-rational, giving a point
a\in[\mathrm{X}^{rss}\sslash\mathrm{H}](k)
. By Assumption
7.15
, there is a class
\xi\in H^{1}(k,\mathrm{H})
, represented by a
1
-cocycle
h:\Gamma\longrightarrow\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
such that if we denote the twisted Galois action on
{\mathrm{G}}(\overline{k})
(resp.
\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
) by
\sigma_{\xi}
, we obtain a pure inner twist
({\mathrm{G}}_{\xi},\theta_{\xi})
such that
\mathrm{H}_{\xi}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\xi}^{\theta_{\xi}})^{\circ}
and there exists an element
h\in\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
such that
hxh^{-1}\in\mathrm{X}_{\xi}^{rss}(k)\subset{\mathrm{G}}_{\xi}(k)
lies over the point
a\in[\mathrm{X}^{rss}\sslash\mathrm{H}](k)
. Note that since
h_{\sigma}\in\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
, the involution
\theta:{\mathrm{G}}\to{\mathrm{G}}
commutes with
\sigma_{\xi}
for all
\sigma\in\Gamma
, so descends to an involution on
{\mathrm{G}}_{\xi}
over
k
, which we have denoted by
\theta_{\xi}
.

Unwinding the claim that
hxh^{-1}
is rational gives the equation

(7.5)

\displaystyle x=\Ad(h^{-1}{}^{\sigma_{\xi}}h)\Ad(h_{\sigma}n_{\sigma}^{-1})(x),

so that
(h^{-1}{}^{\sigma_{\xi}}h)\cdot(h_{\sigma}n_{\sigma}^{-1})\in Z_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k}).

Note that this implies that the cocycle
\sigma\in\Gamma\mapsto h^{-1}h_{\sigma}{}^{\sigma}h
is valued in
N_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})
and lies over
w:\Gamma\longrightarrow W_{\mathrm{X}}
with respect to the map

H^{1}(k,N_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-}))\longrightarrow H^{1}(k,W_{\mathrm{X}}).

Returning to the map
j_{-}:T^{-}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{A}^{-}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
over
\overline{k}
, if we twist the Galois action on
{\mathrm{G}}
by
h_{\sigma}
, we obtain

{}^{\sigma_{\xi}}j_{-}=\Ad(h_{\sigma}n_{\sigma}^{-1})\circ j_{-}

It now follows from equation (
7.5
) that the conjugate
\Ad(h)\circ j_{-}
descends to a
k
-rational embedding
T^{-}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}_{\xi}
. Since
h\in\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
, it follows that s
hj_{-}(T^{-})h^{-1}
is a maximally
\theta_{\xi}
-split torus.
∎

This is an analogue of Kottwitz’s embedding statement for maximal tori and quasi-split groups
[
Kot82
, Lemma 2.2]
. In the relative setting, we transfer flats on
\mathrm{X}
rather than tori themselves and it need not suffice to consider the quasi-split form alone. On the other hand, an easy adaptation of the proof of
loc. cit.
applies whenever
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash\Res_{E/k}(\mathrm{H}_{E})
is a Galois symmetric variety with
\mathrm{H}
quasi-split.

Lemma 7.18
.

Let
k
be a perfect field, and let
E/k
be a quadratic extension. Assume that
\mathrm{H}
is a quasi-split reductive
k
-group and set
{\mathrm{G}}=\Res_{E/k}(\mathrm{H}_{E})
. Set
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
for the associated Galois symmetric variety. For any
\Gamma
-invariant
{\mathrm{G}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
-conjugacy class of maximally
\vartheta
-split maximal torus
\check{T}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}(=\check{{\mathrm{G}}})
, there exists a
k
-rational embedding
{T}\to{\mathrm{G}}
realizing
{T}
as a maximally
{\theta}
-split torus.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{A}
be a
k
-rational maximally
\theta
-split maximal torus of
{\mathrm{G}}
. Arguing as above, the assumptions give a
{\theta}
-equivariant
\overline{k}
-isomorphisms

{j}_{-}:{T}^{-}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{A}}^{-}\subset{\mathrm{G}},\qquad{j}_{+}:{T}^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{A}}^{+}\subset{\mathrm{G}}

of
\theta
-split tori.
As before, there exists a lift
n_{\sigma}\in N_{{\mathrm{G}}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})
such that
{}^{\sigma}j_{\pm}=\Ad(n_{\sigma})^{-1}\circ j_{\pm}
. Since
Z_{{\mathrm{G}}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})=\mathrm{A}
in this setting, there is an inclusion
W_{\mathrm{X}}\subset W
, so we may further assume that the cochain
\sigma\mapsto n_{\sigma}
takes values in
N_{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{A}^{-})(\overline{k})\cap N_{\mathrm{G}}(\mathrm{A})(\overline{k})
.

In this case, the maximal
\theta
-fixed torus
\mathrm{A}^{+}\subset\mathrm{A}
is a maximal torus of
\mathrm{H}
. Applying Lemma 2.1 of
[
Kot82
]
to
\mathrm{H}
implies that there exists
h\in\mathrm{H}(\overline{k})
such that

h^{-1}\sigma(h)n_{\sigma}^{-1}\in j(T)(\overline{k}).

One now checks that
\Ad(h)\circ j:T\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}
is
k
-rational and realizes
hj(T)h^{-1}
as a maximally
\theta
-split torus
∎

8.
Endoscopic data for symmetric varieties

Let
{\mathrm{G}}
be a connected reductive group over
k
. In this section, we use the previous results on outer forms and the dual symmetric variety to develop a notion of endoscopic data for a symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
. After recalling the basic notions, we state our main existence theorem in Theorem
8.5
. We formulate the definitions in Section
8.3
. Finally, Section
8.4
proves the matching of geometric semi-simple orbits between an endoscopic symmetric variety and
\mathrm{X}
in Theorem
8.13
.

8.1.
Endoscopic data

We first recall the general notion of endoscopic triples as in
[
Kot86
,
Kal19
]
. In Section
10.2
and
[
Les24
]
, it is necessary to work with pure inner forms of a given group
{\mathrm{G}}
, so we work with the appropriate version of endoscopic triple.

Definition 8.1
.

A
pure-refined endoscopic datum
is a triple
{\mathfrak{e}}=({\mathrm{G}}_{{\mathfrak{e}}},\kappa,\eta)
where
{\mathrm{G}}_{{\mathfrak{e}}}
is a pure inner form of a quasi-split reductive group
{\mathrm{G}}_{{\mathfrak{e}}}^{\ast}
over
k
,
\kappa\in Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})
is semi-simple and central, and

\eta:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}}

is an algebraic morphism of dual groups subject to the conditions

(1)

\eta(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\eta(\kappa)}^{\circ}
,

(2)

the
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
-conjugacy class of
\eta
is
\Gamma
-fixed, and

(3)

\kappa\in Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})^{\Gamma}
.

An isomorphism of such triples is an isomorphism of algebraic groups
f:{\mathrm{G}}^{\ast}_{{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}^{\ast}_{{\mathfrak{e}}_{2}}
defined over
k
such that

(1)

\eta_{1}\circ\check{f}
and
\eta_{2}
are
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
-conjugate.

(2)

The images of
\check{f}(\kappa_{2})
and
\kappa_{1}
in
\pi_{0}(Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{{\mathfrak{e}}_{1}})^{\Gamma})
coincide.

For any such triple, there is a canonical inclusion
Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}})\subset Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})
, and we say that
{\mathfrak{e}}
is
elliptic
if
Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})^{\Gamma,\circ}\subset Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}})
.

Remark 8.2
.

The definition of endoscopic triples in
[
Kot84
]
takes
{\kappa}\in[Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})/Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}})]^{\Gamma}
such that the image of
\tilde{\kappa}
in
H^{1}(\Gamma,Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}))
is trivial for
k
local and everywhere locally trivial when
k
is global. When working with non-quasi-split groups
{\mathrm{G}}
which are pure inner forms of a quasi-split form
{\mathrm{G}}^{\ast}
, It becomes necessary to fix a lift in
Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})^{\Gamma}
as above.

8.2.
Endoscopic varieties

Now let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be a symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety associated to an
k
-rational involution
\theta
of
{\mathrm{G}}
. We assume that
{\mathrm{G}}
is quasi-split.

Remark 8.3
.

The assumption that
{\mathrm{G}}
be quasi-split is not essential. Extending to a general
{\mathrm{G}}
will require an adaptation of rigid inner forms
[
Kal16
]
to the relative setting. This will be the subject of future work.

Let
(\mathrm{A},\B)
be a
(\theta,k)
-admissible pair. Then
\mathrm{A}
is maximally
\theta
-split and we have the exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\widetilde{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow 1.

We set
\T_{\mathrm{X}}=(\widetilde{\T}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\circ}
. This induces the exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(\widetilde{\T}_{\mathrm{X}})^{D}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}\longrightarrow\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow 1.

Following Proposition
7.10
, there exists an involution
\vartheta:\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
and a unique
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}
-conjugacy class of distinguished morphism

\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}},

such that
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}=(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\vartheta,\circ}
, independent of the choice of
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Fix one such map
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
and set
\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. We let
{x}_{0}\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}
denote the tautological base point fixed by
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
. We also have the finite map

\hat{s}=\hat{s}_{\mathrm{X}}:\hat{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\vartheta}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}{\longrightarrow}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}},

which is a closed immersion when
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\vartheta}_{\mathrm{X}}
is connected. We shall refer to this as the symmetrization map for
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
.

Recall that
\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
is a Levi subgroup determined by
\Delta^{p}_{\mathrm{X}}
satisfies that the inclusion
\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\cap\mathrm{H}\subset\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}
is normal and there is a short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\cap\mathrm{H}\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow 1.

Setting
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}=\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})
and
\mathrm{M}_{\mathrm{X}}:=(\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\cap\mathrm{H})^{\circ}
, this induces a commutative diagram with exact rows
[
Kot84
, Section 1.8]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

where the bottom row follows from the normality of the map
\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}\cap\mathrm{H}\to\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Moreover, it is shown in
[
KS17
, Theorem 9.12]
that
\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is the centralizer of
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
in
{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
, so the above sequence specializes to

1\longrightarrow{\mathrm{A}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow Z(\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}})\longrightarrow Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})\longrightarrow 1.

In particular, this gives a map
Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})\to\check{\mathrm{X}}
via
\hat{\tau}
satisfying that

(8.1)

\mathcal{A}_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}}\simeq\check{s}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})\cap Z(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})\hookrightarrow Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}}).

Suppose now that
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}(\mathbb{C})
is semi-simple. There exists a
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
-conjugate
x_{0}
of
x
lying in
\check{\mathrm{A}}

[
Ric82
, Theorem 7.5]
, so we may assume that
x
lies in the flat
\check{\tau}(\check{\mathrm{A}})\simeq\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Note that this may not be a
\Gamma
-equivariant twist. Then
\hat{s}(x)
is semi-simple as an element of
{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
and
\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Considering the centralizer
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{x}:=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\hat{s}(x)}
, we obtain a diagram

(8.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{x},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X},x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\tau}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}}

where
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}_{x}
are the connected components of the identity of the corresponding centralizers, the involution
\vartheta
preserves
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, and
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}\subset\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is defined so that

[\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}]^{\vartheta,\circ}={\mathrm{G}}_{\mathrm{X},x}^{\ast}:=\varphi_{\mathrm{X},x}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x})

and the diagram

(8.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X},x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\tau}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\tau}}

commutes.
The following lemma is an immediate check.

Lemma 8.4
.

The subgroup
\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X},x}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}_{x}
is a Levi subgroup equal to the centralizer of
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
in
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{x}
.
If
\check{s}(x)\in Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})
, then

\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X},x}=\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}_{x}.

Recall that
\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{ss}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}\cdot\hat{s}^{-1}(\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}})
by
[
Ric82
, Proposition 6.3]
. Motivated by the preceding lemma and the inclusion
Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})\subset\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}
, we let
\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit}:=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}\cdot\hat{s}^{-1}(Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}}))
be the subvariety of semi-simple
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{ss}
such that
\hat{s}(x)
is centralized by a
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
-conjugate of
\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. More generally, let
S\subset{\mathrm{G}}
be a maximally
\theta
-split maximal torus and let
j:\check{S}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
be a representative of the canonical
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
-orbit of embeddings of
\check{S}
as a maximally
\vartheta
-split torus (cf. Lemma
7.14
). We note that
\check{S}^{\heartsuit}:=\check{S}\cap[j^{-1}\circ\check{\tau}^{-1}(\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit})]
is independent of the choice of
j
.

8.2.1.
Existence

We show how to produce symmetric varieties of endoscopic groups of
{\mathrm{G}}
from
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
; the critical point is existence and uniqueness up to
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner forms of such varieties.

Let
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit}
and let
g\in\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
such that
g\cdot\hat{s}(x)\in Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})\subset\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}
. We assume further that
\sigma(x)=x
for all
\sigma\in\Gamma
7
7

7

This assumption is intended to be compatible with the notion of
pure refined endoscopic data
discussed above. More generally, we may allow
\check{s}(x)\in Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})
to project to a
\Gamma
-invariant element in
Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})/Z(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})
.
.
Let

\hat{\eta}_{x}:\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
and
\eta_{x}:{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}_{x}\subset{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}

denote the inclusions of connected components of the identities of the two centralizers. Recall that the
L
-action of
\Gamma
on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
induces a unique action on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, and we denote the corresponding semi-direct product as
{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. The assumption that
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit,\Gamma}
implies that the centralizers are
\Gamma
-stable, so that the maps above extend to maps

{}^{L}\hat{\eta}_{x}:\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{x}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}\text{ and }{}^{L}\eta_{x}:\mathcal{G}_{x}\longrightarrow{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}},

where

1\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{x}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{x}\longrightarrow\Gamma\longrightarrow 1

is the extension induced by the endoscopic datum
\mathfrak{e}_{x}
, and similarly for
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{x}
. These extensions need not be the
L
-group of a reductive group over
k
. Note that
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is stable under
\vartheta
and
\Gamma
. Let

denote the quasi-split reductive group over

dual to

in the sense of
[

, so that
\mathfrak{e}_{x}:=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\hat{s}(x),\eta)
gives a (not-necessarily elliptic) pure-refined endoscopic triple of
{\mathrm{G}}
.

Theorem 8.5
.

Assume that
\mathrm{X}
is well-adapted. Let
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit,\Gamma}
be as above. Let

be the (quasi-split) endoscopic group associated to

. There exists an

-rational involution

and a
k

such that if
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}:=\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is the associated symmetric variety,

(1)

is a

-admissible torus with

the corresponding quotient torus, there exists a

-rational embedding

realizing

as a maximally

-split torus of a pure inner twist

;

(2)

a
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
-conjugate of

identifies the sequence of connected reductive groups

of the

-variety

with the top row of (
8.3
);

(3)

there is a canonical morphism

.

If Conjecture
3.17
holds for
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
, then
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
may be chosen so that the geometric
1
-cocycle

constructed in Definition
3.19
is cohomologous to

defined by

(8.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})(\overline{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})(\overline{k}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\mathrm{X},\mathfrak{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dist}_{\mathfrak{e}}}

Such a variety

is unique up to

-rational isomorphism if

is trivial.

Proof.

Let us first assume that
\mathrm{H}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
. Using the notation from Section
5.4
, the involution
\theta
on
{\mathrm{G}}
induces an admissible
(\Gamma,\theta)
-index
\I=(X^{\ast}(\mathrm{A}),\Delta,\emptyset,\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{p},\sigma_{\ast},\theta^{\ast})
, and the data

determines the combinatorial spherical datum
\Omega_{\mathrm{X}}
. For ease of notation, we conflate
\hat{s}(x)
with

.

Suppose that
(\check{\mathrm{A}},\check{\B})
is a
\Gamma
-stable Borel pair for
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. We further assume that
\check{\mathrm{A}}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is maximally
\vartheta
-split. Let (
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\check{\B}_{\mathfrak{e}})
be similarly defined for
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. Replacing
\hat{s}(x)
with
gxg^{-1}\in Z(\check{\mathrm{M}}_{\mathrm{X}})
as above (which amounts to conjugating everything), we may assume that

and

. Note now that

is no longer
\Gamma

. Since

,

is
\vartheta
-equivariant when restricted to
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},x}
. We thus obtain a
\vartheta
-equivariant identification

X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}})\simeq X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}})

such that the
\Gamma
-actions differ by a
1
-cocycle
w\in Z^{1}(\Gamma,W^{\vartheta})
, where
W^{\vartheta}
is the fixed points of
\vartheta
. Noted that if

is the little Weyl group for
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
relative to the maximally
\vartheta
-split maximal torus
\check{\mathrm{A}}
, then
W_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}}\simeq W^{\vartheta}/W_{2}
with

. This follows since the cocycle takes values in the Weyl group for
(\check{{\mathrm{G}}},\check{\mathrm{A}})
, commutes with
\vartheta
, and
[
Ric82
, Section 4]
.

In particular, we may assume that
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is maximally
\vartheta
-split and that

is
\vartheta
-stable.
Recall that there is a
k
-rational inclusion
\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}:={\mathrm{G}}_{x}
, so that the root system

for

is contained in

. Let

be a

-rational Borel containing

and inducing the basis

corresponding to

. Setting

to be the

-rational involution on

-\vartheta:X^{\ast}({\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}})=X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}})\longrightarrow X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}})=X^{\ast}({\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}}),

we claim that

-basis. Indeed, this follows from the fact that

over

,

\theta^{\ast}=-w_{\mathrm{X}}\theta
is the induced diagram automorphism, which is well-defined as the Weyl elements

.

That this index is admissible is precisely the statement of Proposition
4.10
.

In particular, there exists a
k
-rational involution

on

) and (

) for any choice of

with

-action.

Using our

-equivariant identification

of

Since
Z({\mathrm{G}})\subset Z({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}})
, this descends to a morphism
\Out_{\mathrm{X}}(\mathrm{H})\to\Out_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}(\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}})

.

We thereby obtain a
1
-cocycle

\mu_{\mathrm{X},\mathfrak{e}}:\Gamma\overset{\mu_{\mathrm{X}}}{\longrightarrow}\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})(\overline{k})\overset{\mathrm{dist}_{\mathfrak{e}}}{\longrightarrow}{\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})}(\overline{k}).

Assuming Conjecture
3.17
holds for
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
, the class

As discussed in Section

, this implies that there exists a

be a

-isomorphism, so that there exists

such that

It is now easy to verify that

where the conjugated involution

\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\prime}(g)=s(\theta(s^{-1}gs))s^{-1},\quad\text{where }g\in{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\overline{k})

is defined over

. Replacing

with an element of the form

with

), we may assume that

, and

. This procedure does not change the index

, up to isomorphism. Thus, replacing

and

with

and

-cocycle

satisfies (

). This completes the proof when

.

Now consider the case that
\mathrm{H}=({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}
. There is a natural
{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant dominant morphism

, so by functoriality
[

, Theorem 1]
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}}

However, since
\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}_{\theta}
is an isogeny,
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}}=\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
so that

. Thus,

corresponds a unique element
x^{\prime}\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\theta}
. Running the preceding argument for the group
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
we obtain the involution

; setting
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}:={\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
, this induces a canonical embedding

. Recall now the notation from Section
B
: passing to the group

induces a short exact sequence of
\Gamma
-modules

0\to X^{\ast}_{\theta}/\Lambda_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow X^{\ast}_{\theta,\circ}/\Lambda_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow X^{\ast}_{\theta,\circ}/X^{\ast}_{\theta}\longrightarrow 0,

dual to the sequence

1\longrightarrow\pi_{0}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}\longrightarrow 1.

We obtain a similar short exact sequence for
\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}=({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})^{\circ}
, with
\pi_{0}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})
dual to

. We now show that

X^{\ast}_{\theta,\circ}\subset X^{\ast}_{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\circ}.

Using
[
Hof18
, Corollary 1.5]
, this follows by showing that if
\lambda\in X^{\ast}_{\theta,\mathbb{Q}}\cap\mathrm{X}^{\ast}(\mathrm{A})
satisfies that
\langle\rho(D),\lambda\rangle\in\mathbb{Z}
for all colors
D\in\mathcal{D}({\mathrm{X}_{\theta}})
, then the same holds for all
D\in\mathcal{D}({{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash G_{\mathfrak{e}}})
. To see this, note that since
\Lambda_{{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\subset\Lambda_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}
, we have

\check{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}\subset\check{\Lambda}_{{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}.

By Proposition
B.7
,
\rho(\mathcal{D}({\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}))=\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}^{n}
is the set of coroots for the simple relative roots by Lemma
B.2
and similarly for
\rho(\mathcal{D}({{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}))=\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{n}
. Since
\Delta_{\mathfrak{e}}\subset\Phi^{+}
, it follows that

\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{n}\subset\{\alpha-\theta(\alpha):\alpha\in\Phi\}\setminus\{0\}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{\theta}.

Since
\lambda\in X^{\ast}_{\theta,\mathbb{Q}}\cap\mathrm{X}^{\ast}(\mathrm{A})
pairs integrally with the coroots associated to elements of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\theta}
, it follows that it pairs integrally with
\rho(\mathcal{D}({{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}))
.

This implies that there is a unique subgroup
({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})^{\circ}\subset\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\subset{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
satisfying that if
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
, then (
1
) and (
2
) hold. We note that a similar argument now holds for any
({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
by replacing
X^{\ast}_{\theta,\circ}
with the appropriate sublattice. In particular,
G^{\theta}/\mathrm{H}\simeq{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}/\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}
.

Finally, if
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset N_{\mathrm{G}}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
, we may similarly reduce to the case of
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
. More precisely, there is a natural
{\mathrm{G}}
-equivariant quotient
\mathrm{X}_{\theta}:={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}\to\mathrm{X}
, so by functoriality there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}_{\theta}}

so that
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}
induces a unique element
x^{\prime}\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\theta}
. Running the preceding argument we obtain the involution
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}:{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. The group
\mathrm{H}
corresponds to the
k
-rational subgroup

(8.5)

\mathrm{H}/{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}\subset\mathcal{A}_{{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}},

Thus there exists a unique
k
-rational subgroup
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\subset\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\subset N_{{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}})
corresponding to

. By construction, the variety
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
satisfies properties (
1
) and (

) of the proposition. It is also immediate that property (

) holds since it holds for
{{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}}
.
∎

For
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}
as in the theorem, the
k
-rational symmetric pair constructed will be referred to as an
endoscopic symmetric pair

({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}})
, and
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
as the associated endoscopic symmetric variety. In this generality, there need not exist a
k
-form of
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
that is uniquely distinguished by the construction, even up to
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner form. A first constraint is whether Conjecture
3.17
holds for
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. Let us assume this conjecture for the moment.

It is generally not possible to isolate a single endoscopic variety up to isomorphism; see Example
8.12
below. This is reminiscent of the issue of the
L
-group
{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}}
determining
{\mathrm{G}}
only up to inner twist, and motivates Theorem
6.4
.

Corollary 8.6
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
,
\mathrm{X}
,
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
, and
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
are as in Theorem

. There is a
unique
(up to isomorphism)
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
-form satisfying (
8.4
) and such that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\circ}
is quasi-split.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Theorem
6.4
.
∎

We note that it is possible for
\mathrm{X}
to be well-adapted, while
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is not (see Table
4
).

8.3.
Endoscopic data for symmetric varieties

We now assume that
k
is a global, local, or finite field. We continue to assume that
\mathrm{char}(k)
is good for
{\mathrm{G}}
.
Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is a connected reductive group over
k
. Let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be a symmetric
k
-variety which we assume satisfies Assumption
6.21
(so that
\mathrm{X}
is both well-adapted and that Conjecture
3.17
holds for
\mathrm{X}
).

Let
(\mathrm{A},\B)
be a
\theta
-admissible pair. This induces
\Gamma
-stable Borel subgroups for
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
,
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
and
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
denoted
\check{\B},

\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
, and
\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
, respectively. Moreover,
\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
is
\vartheta
-stable and for any choice of distinguished morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
compatible with
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{\mathrm{A}}
, we have
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}})\subset\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Fix a choice of pinning
\{x_{\check{\alpha}}\}
for
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
, so that
\Gamma
acts as explained in Section

.

Definition 8.7
.

We define an
endoscopic datum
of
\mathrm{X}
to be a quintuple

{\mathfrak{e}}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,\eta)

where
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is an endoscopic group equipped with a
k
-rational involution
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}
and a symmetric variety
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
associated to
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}
satisfying the requirements of Theorem
8.5
such that

(1)

if
\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}:\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\subset\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is the symmetrization map of the dual variety, then
({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\kappa),\eta)
gives a pure refined endoscopic datum for
{\mathrm{G}}
,

(2)

\eta
restricts to an isomorphism
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\simeq(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\eta(\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\kappa))})^{\circ}
and intertwines the involutions
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}}
and
\vartheta
,

(3)

\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\kappa)\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\heartsuit}
and
\eta(\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\kappa))\in\hat{s}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})^{\heartsuit}\subset\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
,

(4)

the dual variety
\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
fits into a commutative diagram

(8.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}

Here,
\eta_{\kappa}
realizes
\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
as the
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
-orbit at
\eta(\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\kappa))
and

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\overset{\varphi_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}{\longrightarrow}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}

is the unique distinguished morphism associated to
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
such that the diagram commutes.

We say that
\mathfrak{e}
is
elliptic
if
\mathcal{A}_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{\Gamma,\circ}=\mathcal{A}_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}}^{\Gamma,\circ}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}}=\Aut^{\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})
is the
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
-equivariant automorphism group of
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
is defined similarly.

Remark 8.8
.

For any distinguished morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
, we have
\ker(\varphi_{\mathrm{X}})=\ker(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{\T})

[
KS17
, Remark 7.8]
. In particular, for a given endoscopic datum
{\mathfrak{e}}
and morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
, the morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}^{\mathfrak{e}}}
is uniquely determined by the commutativity of (
8.6
) and has the same kernel as
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Definition 8.9
.

An isomorphism of such data

\mathfrak{e}_{1}=({\mathrm{G}}_{1},\theta_{1},\mathrm{X}_{1},\kappa_{1},\eta_{1})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{e}_{2}=({\mathrm{G}}_{2},\theta_{2},\mathrm{X}_{2},\kappa_{2},\eta_{2})

is an isomorphism
f:({\mathrm{G}}_{1},\hat{s}_{1}(\kappa_{1}),\eta_{1})\to({\mathrm{G}}_{2},\hat{s}_{2}(\kappa_{2}),\eta_{2})
of the endoscopic triples satisfying

(1)

f
intertwines
\theta_{1}
with a pure inner twist of
\theta_{2}
. That is, the involution
\theta_{2}^{\prime}:=f^{-1}\circ\theta_{1}\circ f
is a pure inner twist of
\theta_{2}
;

(2)

the morphism
\eta_{1}\circ\check{f}
and
\eta_{2}
are
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}}
-conjugate;

(3)

the images
\check{f}(\kappa_{2})
and
\kappa_{1}
coincide in
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{1}}^{\Gamma})
.

Let
\Aut(\mathfrak{e})
denote the automorphism group of the endoscopic datum. Note that there is a natural inclusion
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}/(\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\cap Z({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}))(k)\subset\Aut(\mathfrak{e})
, which we refer to as the group of inner automorphism.

We remark that the requirement that
f
intertwines
\theta_{1}
with a pure inner twist of
\theta_{2}
forces
f
to descend to an isomorphism

\mathrm{X}_{1}=\mathrm{H}_{1}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{1}\longrightarrow f(\mathrm{H}_{1})\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{2}\simeq\mathrm{H}_{2}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{2}=\mathrm{X}_{2}.

This follows from Lemma
5.1
.

Remark 8.10
.

While the definition of an endoscopic datum includes the involution, we see that this involution is only determined up to pure inner twist. In light of Lemma
5.1
, the data of
[\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}]
is encoded by
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. We nevertheless find it psychologically nicer to keep track of the involution.

Definition 8.11
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is a connected reductive group over
k
. Let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be a symmetric
k
-variety which we assume is well-adapted. Let
{\mathfrak{e}}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,\eta)
denote an endoscopic datum for
({\mathrm{G}},\theta,\mathrm{X})
. We say that
\mathfrak{e}
is the
quasi-split endoscopic datum
if
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is the quasi-split form of Corollary
8.6
.

We will always assume that for a given endoscopic datum
\mathfrak{e}
that
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is the quasi-split form; this only plays a role in Section
10.2
.

Theorem
8.5
shows that for any
\kappa\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit,\Gamma}
, there exists an endoscopic datum
\mathfrak{e}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,\eta)
containing
\kappa
. On the other hand, Lemma
2.7
implies that any two endoscopic varieties
\mathrm{X}_{1}=\mathrm{H}_{1}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
and
\mathrm{X}_{2}=\mathrm{H}_{2}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
associated to
\kappa
in this fashion satisfy that
\mathrm{H}_{1}
and
\mathrm{H}_{2}
are inner twists of each other by a cocycle with values in
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. In particular, the construction of Theorem
8.5
does not distinguish between non-isomorphic
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
-inner forms
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1}
and
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},2}
in general.

Example 8.12
.

Let
{\mathrm{G}}=\Res_{E/k}(\mathrm{H}_{E})
and
\mathrm{H}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
is the fixed point subgroup of a Galois involution. If
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
is any inner form of
\mathrm{H}
such that
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{E}\simeq\mathrm{H}
but
\mathrm{H}^{\prime}
is not a pure inner twist, then the varieties
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
are
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner twists of each other and not isomorphic. Theorem
8.5
cannot distinguish between such pairs.

8.3.1.
Extended endoscopic data

For the purposes of (relative) functoriality, it is necessary to work with morphisms of
L
-groups rather than dual groups. In this subsection, we discuss the problem of enhancing an endoscopic datum
\mathfrak{e}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,\eta)
to be compatible with
L
-groups. Following the discussion of the
\Gamma
-actions on dual groups in Section
7.1
, we may associate to the symmetric pair
({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})
the sequence of groups

{}^{L}\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\longrightarrow{}^{L}{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}},

extending the maps on dual groups. Recall that the
\Gamma
-action on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
is completely determined by that on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
, and that the action on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
is so determined once the convention (
7.1
) is imposed. In particular, if
\eta
extends to an
L
-homomorphism
{}^{L}\eta
, we obtain an embedding

(8.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\eta}

In this case, we refer to
{}^{L}\mathfrak{e}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,{}^{L}\eta)
as an extended endoscopic datum. This is always possible if
{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
is simply connected
[
Lan79
, Proposition 1]
; more generally, one makes use of a
z
-extension to reduce to this case.

If we fix a compatible
z
-extension
({\mathrm{G}}_{1},\theta_{1})
of
({\mathrm{G}},\theta)
with

1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}\longrightarrow 1,

then by Remark
7.7
and Lemma
5.8
we have (for appropriately chosen distinguished morphisms) a commutative diagram

(8.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{N/N^{\theta_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{N}^{\theta_{1}},}

where the bottom row is dual to the short exact sequence of induced
k
-tori
