1\longrightarrow N^{\theta_{1}}\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow N/N^{\theta_{1}}\longrightarrow 1,

and the right-most vertical column is a fibration.
In particular, to an
x\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{ss}
, we obtain
x_{1}\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{1}^{ss}
via the induced inclusion. Notice that
x_{1}
depends only on the choice of compatible
z
-extension since
\hat{\mathrm{X}}
,
\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{1}
, and the inclusions are independent of the choice of distinguished morphisms. One may similarly obtain a
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}
-compatible
z
-extension
({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1},\theta_{\mathfrak{e},1})
such that

1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow 1,

which fits into an extended endoscopic datum
{}^{L}\mathfrak{e}_{1}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1},\theta_{\mathfrak{e},1},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1},x_{1},{}^{L}\eta_{1})
so that there is an embedding

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e},1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}}_{1},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\eta_{1}}

Such extensions play a similar role to
z
-extensions in the formulation of the matching of orbital integrals as explained in
[
Les24
]
.
Indeed, setting
N_{\theta}:={N/N^{\theta_{1}}}
we obtain an exact sequence

{}^{L}\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1}\to{}^{L}\mathrm{X}_{1}\to{N_{\theta}}\rtimes\Gamma.

Restricting the first morphism to
\Gamma
and pulling back to the Weil group
W_{k}\to\Gamma
this gives a map

\check{\lambda}:W_{k}\to{N_{\theta}}\rtimes W_{k}.

When
k
is local, this gives a parameter for a character
\lambda:N_{\theta}(k)\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The main point is that if
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathrm{X}(k))
, we may view it as a function on
\mathrm{X}_{1}(k)
constant on
N_{\theta}
-orbits. Then the appropriate
\kappa
-orbital integrals of
f
should match with the stable orbital integrals of a function
f^{\mathfrak{e},1}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}(k),\lambda)
, where we view
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1}
as a
N_{\theta}
-torsor satisfying that
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)\to\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}(k)
is surjective since
N_{\theta}
is an induced torus. We then form the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
-bundle
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\lambda}=\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)\times^{N_{\theta}(k)}\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{\lambda}
and consider compactly-supported sections of the associated line bundle

C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}(k),\lambda)=\bigoplus_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e},1}^{\prime}\in\ker^{1}(\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e},1},{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1};k)}\ind_{\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)N(k)}^{{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)}(\overline{\lambda}),

where
\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e},1}^{\prime}
runs over a set of pure inner twists of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e},1}
indexed by
\ker^{1}(\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e},1},{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1};k)
and

\ind_{\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)N(k)}^{{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)}(\overline{\lambda})=\{f:{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)\to\mathbb{C}:f(nhg)=\overline{\lambda}(n)f(g)\},

where
n\in N(k),\;h\in\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k),\>g\in{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)
,
\mathrm{supp}(f)\text{ is compact modulo }\mathrm{H}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e},1}(k)N(k),
and
\overline{\lambda}:N(k)\to N_{\theta}(k)\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is the pull-back of
\lambda
to
N(k)
.

8.4.
Point matching

The following theorem gives a matching of stable semi-simple orbits of symmetric varieties.

Theorem 8.13
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is a quasi-split reductive group over
k
. Let
\theta
be a
k
-rational involution and let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be an associated symmetric variety. Let
\mathfrak{e}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,\eta)
be an endoscopic datum for
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
. There exists a canonical map

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}:\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}

between the categorical quotients.

A direct consequence of this is the ability to compare objects indexed by stable orbits (such as stable orbital integrals) between
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
and
\mathrm{X}
; see Section
10.2
.

We begin with the following simplification.

Lemma 8.14
.

Suppose that the preceding theorem holds whenever
\mathrm{H}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
. Then it holds for all symmetric subgroups.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{A}
be a fixed maximally
\theta
-split maximal torus, so that
s:\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}_{\theta}
is the quotient to the canonical torus of
\mathrm{X}_{\theta}:={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
. For any
({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset N_{{\mathrm{G}}}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
, Richardson gives an isomorphism of
k
-schemes
[
Ric82
, Corollary 11.5]

(8.9)

\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}\simeq\mathrm{A}_{\theta}/W_{\mathrm{X}},

where
W_{\mathrm{X}}
denotes the little Weyl group of
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}_{\theta}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
.

Suppose now that
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset N_{{\mathrm{G}}}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
. By the proof of Theorem
8.5
and the notation therein, we obtain an involution
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}
on
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
such that there is a unique
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\subset\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}
satisfying the desired properties of the theorem. By assumption there is a a canonical map

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta}:\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}

between the categorical quotients. The Chevalley-type isomorphism (
8.9
) implies that this in fact gives an isomorphism

\mathfrak{a}_{\theta}:\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}

The inclusion of groups
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\subset\mathrm{H}
induces a unique morphism
\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}\to\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}
realizing
\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}
as the categorical quotient of
\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}
by the automorphism group

\mathcal{Z}:={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\backslash\mathrm{H}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}};

here we rely on Lemma
2.3
with
\mathcal{Z}
corresponding to
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathrm{H}
corresponding to
{\mathrm{G}}
. Let
\pi_{\mathcal{Z}}
denote this quotient map. On the other hand, the inclusion of
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\subset\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}
and the inclusion (
8.5
),
\mathcal{Z}\simeq{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\backslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}
also acts on
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}
with categorical quotient given by
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}
.

Noting that

\pi_{\mathcal{Z}}\circ\mathfrak{a}_{\theta}:\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}

is
\mathcal{Z}
-invariant, it follows that there is a unique morphism
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}:\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}
so that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{a}_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathcal{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}}

commutes. This proves the claim when
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset N_{{\mathrm{G}}}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
.

Remark 8.15
.

Note that

(8.10)

\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}\simeq[\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\theta}]\sslash\mathcal{Z}\simeq\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}},

where
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq\mathrm{A}_{\theta}/\mathcal{Z}
is the canonical torus for
\mathrm{X}
. In particular, the preceding argument extends Richardson’s result to
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
when
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset N_{{\mathrm{G}}}({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})
.

Suppose now that
({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
. Motivated by the preceding remark, we claim that the lemma will follow from extending the Chevalley-type isomorphism to
\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}
by replacing
\mathrm{A}_{\theta}
with its finite cover
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Indeed given such an isomorphism, a similar argument as above gives a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e},\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{A}_{\theta}/W_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{A}_{\theta}/W_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash\mathrm{H}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{a}_{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

It follows that there exists a unique map
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}:\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\to\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}}
so that the diagram commutes.

To prove the claim, we revisit the proof of
[
Ric82
, Theorem 13.2]
: it relies solely on basic algebraic geometry and the following facts:

(1)

Since each element
w\in W_{\mathrm{X}}
has a representative in
N_{K}(\mathrm{A}_{\theta})
, where
K=({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}
, we obtain a morphism of affine varieties

j^{\prime}:\mathrm{A}_{\theta}/W_{\mathrm{X}}\to\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash K

satisfying
\pi_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}\circ j=j^{\prime}\circ\pi_{\mathrm{A}_{\theta}}
, where
j:\mathrm{A}_{\theta}\to\mathrm{X}_{\theta}
is the orbit map. This trivially extends to
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
by the same fact for
W_{\mathrm{X}}
.

(2)

Since
\pi_{\mathrm{X}_{\theta}}
identifies closed
K
-orbits on
\mathrm{X}
with points of
\mathrm{X}_{\theta}\sslash K
, Theorem 7.5 of
[
Ric82
]
implies that
j^{\prime}
is surjective. Since
\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}_{\theta}
is the quotient by a finite subgroup scheme of
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}
with quotient
\mathrm{A}_{\theta}
, this claim immediately extends to
\mathrm{X}
. That is,
x\in\mathrm{X}(\overline{k})
is semi-simple if and only if
K\cdot x
meets
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset\mathrm{X}
.

(3)

Finally, the map
j^{\prime}
is injective by Corollary 11.2 of
[
Ric82
]
. This similarly extends to
\mathrm{X}
by the surjectivity of
\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}_{\theta}
.

Following the argument of
[
Ric82
, Theorem 13.2]
, we therefore conclude the isomorphism (
8.10
) when
({\mathrm{G}}^{\theta})^{\circ}\subset\mathrm{H}\subset{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
and the lemma.

∎

We may thus assume that
\mathrm{H}={\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}={\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}
. Fix
(\theta,k)
-admissible Borel pairs
(\mathrm{A},\B)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}},\B_{\mathfrak{e}}).
This gives rise to a short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow{\mathrm{A}}^{\theta}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow 1,

and a commutative diagram (recall that
\T_{\mathrm{X}}=({\mathrm{A}}^{\theta})^{\circ}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{A}}_{{\mathrm{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{\T}_{\mathrm{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{{\mathrm{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{X}}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{s}}

where
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
is our fixed distinguished morphism. Recall from Proposition
7.10
that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}=\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}})
is the connected component of the identity of
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\vartheta}
. As shown in Section
7.3
, the dual pair
(\check{\mathrm{A}},\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}})
is
\vartheta
-stable, so it gives a fundamental pair for
(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}},\hat{\mathrm{X}})
.

We have a similar diagram for
({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})
and
(\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}},\B_{\mathfrak{e}})
; we let
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}}
denote the corresponding involution on
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
. By assumption, we have a commutative diagram

(8.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\kappa}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{X}}

realizing
(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}})
as the descendant of
(\hat{{\mathrm{G}}},\hat{\mathrm{X}})
at
\kappa\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}
.

Lemma 8.16
.

We may conjugate our choice of endoscopic data
\mathfrak{e}
by an element of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\ast}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
such that we have

(1)

\eta^{-1}(\check{\mathrm{A}},\check{\B})=(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\check{\B}_{\mathfrak{e}})
,

(2)

\eta_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{{\mathrm{X}^{\mathfrak{e}}}})=\check{\mathrm{A}}_{{\mathrm{X}}}
.

In particular, the map
\eta:\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}
may be chosen so that it intertwines
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}}
with
\vartheta
.

Proof.

By the properties of semi-simple descent, the commutativity of the diagram already implies that

(8.12)

\eta\circ\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}}=\vartheta\circ\eta.

In particular, if we set
(\check{\mathrm{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e}},\check{\B}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e}})=\eta^{-1}(\check{\mathrm{A}},\check{\B})
, then
(\check{\mathrm{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e}},\check{\B}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e}})
is a fundamental pair for
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}}
. Since
\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is a spherical variety of minimal rank, Lemma
7.13
implies that there exists
h\in\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{\ast}
such that

\Ad(h)(\check{\mathrm{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e}},\check{\B}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{e}})=(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\check{\B}_{\mathfrak{e}}).

Thus, replacing
\eta
(resp.
\eta_{\mathrm{X}}
) with
\eta\circ\Ad(h^{-1})
(resp.
\eta_{\mathrm{X}}\circ\Ad(h^{-1})
), we may assume that (
1
) holds. With this assumption, (
2
) follows from the commutativity relation (
8.12
) and identifying
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\check{\mathrm{A}}^{\vartheta,\circ}
.
∎

We now assume that
\mathfrak{e}
satisfies the properties of the preceding lemma so that
\eta
induces a
(\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}},\vartheta)
-equivariant isomorphism
X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathfrak{e}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}})
. This induces an
\overline{k}
-isomorphism
\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}:\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{A}
. Lemma
7.9
now implies that
\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}
intertwines the
k
-rational involutions on
\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}
and
\mathrm{A}
:

\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}(\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}(t))=\theta(\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}(t)).

In particular, we see that
\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}
induces an isomorphism

(8.13)

\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{+}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{A}^{+},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{-}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{A}^{-}

between the maximal fixed and split subtori.

The morphism
\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}
need not be a
k
-isomorphism: for each
\sigma\in\Gamma
, and
s\in\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\overline{k})
we obtain

\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}(\sigma_{\mathfrak{e}}(s))=\Ad(w_{\sigma})(\sigma(\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}(s)),

and the map
\sigma\mapsto w_{\sigma}
is a
1
-cocycle valued in
W(\overline{k})
. On the other hand, since the involutions
\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}
and
\theta
are defined over
k
, they commute with the Galois actions on the two tori. It follows that the
1
-cocycle
[w_{\sigma}]\in Z^{1}(\Gamma,W)

preserves the isomorphisms (
8.13
).
This implies that
\{w_{\sigma}\}
is valued in the subgroup
W_{1}=\{w\in W:w(\mathrm{A}^{-})=\mathrm{A}^{-}\}
.

Lemma 8.17
.

Consider the isomorphism
\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}^{-}:\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{-}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{A}^{-},
and let
\{w_{\sigma}\}
denote the
\Gamma
-cocycle valued in
W_{1}(\overline{k})
as above. This descends to a cocycle valued in
W_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq\mathrm{A}^{-}
, so that
W_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq W(\mathrm{H},\mathrm{A}^{-})
. But we have already seen the isomorphism
[
Ric82
, Section 4]

W(\mathrm{H},\mathrm{A}^{-})\simeq W_{1}/W_{2},

where
W_{2}=\{w\in W_{1}:w|_{\mathrm{A}^{-}}\equiv Id\}
.∎

It follows that
\psi_{\mathfrak{e}}
induces a
k
-rational map

\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}/W_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}}.

The Chevalley-style isomorphism for
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
in (
8.9
) thus induces the morphism

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}:\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\simeq\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}/W_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}/W_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq\mathrm{X}\sslash\mathrm{H}.

It is easy to check that
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is independent of all choices, completing the proof of Theorem
8.13
.∎

Corollary 8.18
.

Suppose that
\chi_{\mathfrak{e}}:{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{G}}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}
is the map between categorical quotients of groups. The diagram

(8.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}](k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathrm{X}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}](k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}](k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[{\mathrm{G}}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}](k)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\mathfrak{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi_{\mathfrak{e}}}

commutes. Here the vertical arrows are the natural maps induced by the symmetrization maps.

A similar result – with a simpler argument – follows for the infinitesimal (or Lie algebra) setting. That is, if
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie({\mathrm{G}})
denote the Lie algebra, then
\theta
induces a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading on
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{1}
by setting
\mathfrak{g}_{i}:=\{X\in\mathfrak{g}:d\theta(X)=(-1)^{i}X\}
. Then the
{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}
-representations

T^{\ast}_{x_{0}}\mathrm{X}\simeq\mathfrak{g}_{1}

is referred to as an infinitesimal symmetric variety for
({\mathrm{G}},\theta)
at
x_{0}
.

Proposition 8.19
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is a reductive group over
k
. Let
\theta
be a
k
-rational involution and let
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
be the associated infinitesimal symmetric variety. Let
\mathfrak{e}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,\eta)
be an endoscopic datum for
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
. There exists a canonical
k
-rational morphism

\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{e}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathfrak{e},1}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta_{\mathfrak{e}}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{1}\sslash{\mathrm{G}}^{\theta}.

As noted in the introduction, this result will play a role in any analysis of relative Hitchin fibrations associated to symmetric varieties
[
Les21
,
GPPNR18
,
HM23
]
toward generalizations of Ngô’s results
[
Ngo06
,
Ngô10
]
. We discuss the application of these results toward stabilization of relative trace formulae in Section
10
and prove these results in
[
Les24
]
.

9.
A symplectic representation and the dual Hamiltonian variety

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is a quasi-split connected reductive group over
k
. Let
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
be a symmetric
k
-variety. The goal of this section is to show how, for sufficiently nice symmetric varieties, the geometric cocycle
\mu_{\mathrm{X}}
encodes a symplectic representation of
{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}
so that the resulting data determines
\mathrm{X}
up to
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner form. Stated more directly, we show how to incorporate the outer data
\out_{\mathrm{X}}
on the dual side in terms of a symplectic representation of
{}^{L}\mathrm{X}
. We then explain how to interpret our notion of endoscopic varieties in the context of the relative Langlands duality conjectures and the dual Hamiltonian
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
-variety of Ben-Zvi–Sakellaridis–Venkatesh
[
BZSV23
]
.

9.1.
The symplectic representation
S_{\mathrm{X}}

We now make the assumption that
\mathrm{X}
satisfies Assumption
6.21
and that
\mathrm{H}
is geometrically connected.

Remark 9.1
.

While much of the results in this section can be established in much more generality than this, the statements become difficult to formulate if disconnected groups are allowed. In any case, the assumptions of
[
BZSV23
]
are much more stringent than these. For example, we do not generally impose that
\mathrm{X}
have no type
N
roots until Section
9.2
.

Suppose that
\theta
is a
k
-rational involution of
{\mathrm{G}}
associated to
\mathrm{X}
and let
(\mathrm{A},\B)
be a
(\theta,k)
-admissible pair. We fix a choice of distinguished morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
compatible with
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{\mathrm{A}}
and pinning induced of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. This induces
\Gamma
-stable Borel subgroups for
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
,
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
and
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
denoted
\check{\B},

\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
, and
\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
, respectively. Moreover,
\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
is
\vartheta
-stable and we have
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}(\check{\B})\subset\hat{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})
be the set of
\B
-colors of
\mathrm{X}
and let

\rho\times\varsigma:{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})\longrightarrow X_{\ast}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}})\times\mathcal{P}(\Delta)=X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})\times\mathcal{P}(\check{\Delta})

be the cocharacter map from (
3.2
). Our assumption on
(\mathrm{A},\B)
implies that
\Gamma
acts on
\Delta
and hence on
\mathcal{P}(\check{\Delta})
.

Recall the set
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
of distinguished roots, so that by Theorem
6.1
,

\Out_{\mathrm{X}}(\mathrm{H})\simeq\prod_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2}),

where
I_{\mathrm{X}}
is the set of
\Gamma
-orbits in
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
. Using Proposition
B.7
, there is a corresponding subset
\check{\Sigma}^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset\rho({\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X}))
such that for every
\alpha\in{\Delta}^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}
,
\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}\in\check{\Sigma}^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Definition 9.2
.

We define
\check{\Delta}^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}=\{\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2}\check{\alpha}:\alpha\in{\Delta}^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}\}
by re-scaling elements
\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}
to the unique minimal multiple
\frac{c_{\alpha}}{2}\check{\alpha}\in\check{\mathfrak{X}}^{sv}
.

Remark 9.3
.

Re-normalizing the spherical roots to obtain the lattice
\mathfrak{X}^{sv}=\mathfrak{X}+\mathbb{Z}\Delta^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}
may alter the relationship between
\rho({\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X}))\subset\mathrm{X}^{\ast}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}})
and the representation theory of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
.

We define
S_{\mathrm{X}}
as the unique representation of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
satisfying

S_{\mathrm{X}}=\bigoplus_{\check{\lambda}\in\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{X}}}V(\check{\lambda})\otimes M(\check{\lambda})

where

(1)

\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{X}}=\{\text{highest weights contained in $W_{\mathrm{X}}\cdot\check{\Delta}^{dist}_{\mathrm{X}}$}\},

(2)

the multiplicity space
M(\check{\lambda})
has a basis indexed by colors
D\in{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})
satisfying
\rho(D)=\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}
for any
\check{\alpha}
lying in the
W_{\mathrm{X}}
-orbit of
\frac{2}{c_{\alpha}}\check{\lambda}
.

We will construct an action of
{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}
on
S_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Following the notation from Section
6.4
, there is a canonical decomposition

\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}\mathcal{O}_{i}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}}\bigsqcup_{a\in\Gamma/\Gamma_{i}}a\cdot\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{i}}^{dist}

and for each
i\in I_{\mathrm{X}}
, there is a canonical quadratic character

\mu_{i}:\Gamma_{i}\longrightarrow\{\pm 1\}.

For any
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{i}
, we let
\mu_{\alpha}:\Gamma_{\alpha}\to\{\pm 1\}
be the corresponding character on the stabilizer
\Gamma_{\alpha}=a^{-1}\Gamma_{i}a
. Set
{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})^{dist}\subset{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})
to be those colors
D
such that
\rho(D)\in\check{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
.

Proposition 9.4
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is a symmetric variety satisfying Assumption
6.21
with
\mathrm{H}
geometrically connected. There exists a unique
{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}
-representation on
S_{\mathrm{X}}
extending the algebraic action of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
such that

(1)

there is an isomorphism of
\Gamma
-representations

S_{\mathrm{X}}^{(\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}})}\simeq\mathbb{C}[{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})^{dist}],

where for any
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}\setminus\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}
and
\sigma\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
, we have
\sigma\cdot D_{\alpha}=\mu_{\alpha}(\sigma)D_{\alpha}
where
D_{\alpha}
is the unique color satisfying
\rho(D_{\alpha})=\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}
,

(2)

there is a symplectic structure on
S_{\mathrm{X}}
such that
{}^{L}\mathrm{X}
acts via symplecto-morphisms.

The proof of this relies on the classification of symmetric varieties in a crucial way. In particular, we use the calculation of colors of symmetric varieties in Section
B.2
to reduce to the case of type
C
root systems in the Lemma
9.6
, since these are the only cases where a distinguished root occurs for symmetric varieties. In particular, we exploit a tight relationship between distinguished roots and symplectic quotients of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
.

Remark 9.5
.

As the next lemma shows, this has the added outcome that each irreducible representation
V(\check{\lambda})
occurring in
S_{\mathrm{X}}
is
minuscule
, establishing cases of a conjecture of Ben-Zvi, Sakellaridis, and Venkatesh; see Proposition
9.13
.

The next lemma does not assume that
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{H})
is trivial.

Lemma 9.6
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is quasi-split and
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is a symmetric variety. Fix
{\alpha}\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
and let
\Gamma_{\alpha}\subset\Gamma
denote its stabilizer; set
k_{\alpha}/k
be the associated field extension. Then there exists a unique
k_{\alpha}
-rational reductive normal subgroup
{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathrm{G}}
, stabilized by
\theta
such that

(1)

if
\mathrm{H}_{\alpha}:=\mathrm{H}\cap{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
and
\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}:=\mathrm{H}_{\alpha}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
, there exist surjective morphisms
\pi_{\alpha}
,
\pi_{\alpha,\mathrm{X}}
fitting into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\alpha};}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\alpha,\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}}

(2)

let
\check{\lambda}_{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{X}}
be the unique dominant weight of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
associated to
\alpha
as in (
1
). Then the
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
-action on the highest weight module
V(\check{\lambda}_{\alpha})
factors through
\pi_{\alpha,\mathrm{X}}
.

(3)

Assume that
\mathrm{X}
satisfies Assumption
6.21
. The derived subgroup of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\alpha}
is of type
C
. The corresponding highest weight representation of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is minuscule and symplectic.

Proof.

First note that if
{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}
is a compatible
z
-extension of
{\mathrm{G}}
, we have the morphism
p:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
as in Remark
7.7
with an equality of spherical roots by Lemma
5.8
. Since
{\mathrm{G}}
and
{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}
have isomorphic root lattices and
\check{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathrm{G}}}\subset\check{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}}
, we see that
\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}^{sv}=\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}^{sv}
. This implies that the representation
V(\check{\lambda}_{\alpha})
extends to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
uniquely up to character twist. Finally, it is easy to see that if
{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}
is a reductive group satisfying the properties above, then
{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}:=p({\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}_{\alpha})\subset{\mathrm{G}}
also satisfies the properties. It therefore suffices to prove the claim in the case
{\mathrm{G}}={\mathrm{G}}^{\prime}
has simply-connected derived subgroup.

Suppose
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
. This lemma does not rely on the
k
-rational structure of
{\mathrm{G}}
and
\mathrm{X}
, so we may base change to
k_{\alpha}
and assume that
k=k_{\alpha}
so that
\alpha
is
\Gamma
-fixed.

Recall from (
5.4
) that there is a well-defined
k
-simple sub-root system
\Phi_{\alpha}\subset\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
containing the associated simple root. This corresponds to a
k
-simple factor
{\mathrm{G}}_{der,\alpha}\subset{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
. Since we have assumed that
\alpha
is
\Gamma
-fixed, this implies that
\Phi_{\alpha}
is absolutely simple since otherwise
|\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}^{dist}|>1
by the proof of Theorem
6.1
. Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{G}}_{der}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{G}}_{der,\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha},}

where
{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
is the connected reductive group generated by
{\mathrm{G}}_{der,\alpha}
and
Z({\mathrm{G}})^{\circ}
. Each morphism is normal in the sense of
[
Kot84
, 1.8]
, so that if we fix compatible pinnings for each group, there is a commutative diagram of dual groups given by the front square of (
9.1
) below.

Noting that
\theta
preserves
{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
, we set
\mathrm{H}_{\alpha}:=\mathrm{H}\cap{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
and
\mathrm{H}_{der,\alpha}=\mathrm{H}\cap{{\mathrm{G}}}_{der,\alpha}
. Compatibly with the previous diagram, we have a commuting diagram of injective equivariant morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{der}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{der,\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{X}_{\alpha},}

where
\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}=\mathrm{H}_{\alpha}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
. Fix once and for all pinnings for the four groups and distinguished morphisms for the four varieties (with the natural notations). We claim that there is a commutative cube

(9.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\>\>\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{der}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{der}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\quad\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{der,\alpha}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{der,\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\pi}_{\alpha,\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X},\alpha}}

We need only focus on the back face as every other arrow has been justified. The horizontal arrows are shown to exist in Appendix
A
, while the right-most vertical arrow is straightforward from the decompositions

{\mathrm{G}}_{der}=\prod_{i}{\mathrm{G}}_{der,i}\times\prod_{j}({\mathrm{G}}_{der,j}\times{\mathrm{G}}_{der,j}),\qquad\mathrm{X}_{der}=\prod_{i}\mathrm{X}_{der,i}\times\prod_{j}\mathrm{X}_{der,j}

from Section
5.3
. Thus we need only justify the left vertical (dashed) arrow. Its existence follows from the commutativity of the corresponding diagram of tori. That is, the image of the composition
\check{\pi}_{\alpha}\circ\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
has the same derived subgroup as
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}^{\ast}=\varphi_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}})
. Since the corresponding diagram on dual tori does indeed commute, the composition must factor through
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}
as indicated.

Set
\check{\lambda}_{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{X}}
for the unique dominant weight of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
associated to
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
. Let
\rho_{\alpha}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\GL(V_{\alpha})
be the corresponding representation. We claim that
\rho_{\alpha}
factors through
\check{\pi}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha}:\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}.
Indeed, since the root lattices of
{\mathrm{G}}
and
{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
agree,

\{\alpha\}=\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}^{dist}\subset\bigsqcup_{i}\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{i}}^{sv}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{j}\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{j}}^{sv}=\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{sv}

so that
\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}^{dist}\subset\check{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}
. In particular, since
\pi_{\alpha,\mathrm{X}}
restricts to a quotient of tori
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}
and we have

\check{\lambda}_{\alpha}\in X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha})\subset X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}),

so the representation
V_{\alpha}
factors through the quotient by highest weight theory. This completes the proof of (
1
) and (
2
).

We now impose Assumption
6.21
. Replacing
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
with
({\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha},\mathrm{X}_{\alpha})
, we may thus assume that
{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
is simply connected and absolutely simple. In particular, we may assume
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}=\{\alpha\}
and rely on Lemma
6.20
.

As discussed in Section
B.2
, if
\alpha\in\Delta^{(2)}_{\mathrm{X}}
, then
{\Delta}^{n}_{\mathrm{X}}
is of type
C_{n}
for some
n
such that
2{\alpha}
is the unique long simple root. After normalizing the roots, there are two possibilities: If
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}
is of type
B_{n}
, then
\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}^{\vee}\in\check{\mathfrak{X}}
lies in the
W_{\mathrm{X}}
-orbit of the unique minuscule fundamental weight of the dual group. In this case, the derived subgroup of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is
\Sp_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
for
n=\rk(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}})
and
V(\check{\lambda})
is the standard symplectic representation. On the other hand, if
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}
is of type
C_{n}
, then
\check{\alpha}=\frac{2}{2}\check{\alpha}^{\vee}\in\check{\mathfrak{X}}
lies in the
W_{\mathrm{X}}
-orbit of unique dominant short root of the dual group, which is quasi-minuscule. In this case, the derived subgroup is of type
B_{n}
and
V(\check{\lambda})
is the standard orthogonal representation of
\SO_{2n+1}(\mathbb{C})
. However, this case only occurs for the symmetric varieties of Chevalley type in Lemma
6.20
8
8

8

The difference between
\Spin_{2}\times^{\mu_{2}}\Spin_{2n-1}\backslash\Spin_{2n+1}
and
\Spin_{2}\times^{\mu_{2}}\Spin_{2n-2}\backslash\Spin_{2n}
is the presence a root of type
N
in Lemma
6.8
. In the even case, there are no such roots, and
\alpha
is the short simple root in
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
, implying that
\frac{\check{\alpha}}{2}\in\mathfrak{X}=\mathfrak{X}^{sv}
. In the odd case, renormalization of the spherical root of type
N
ensures that
\alpha
is the
long simple root
of
\Phi^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}
, so that
\frac{\check{\alpha}}{2}\notin\mathfrak{X}^{sv}
.
and therefore does not occur under Assumption
6.21
.

If
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}\setminus\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}
, then
\mathrm{X}
must be a
k
-form of either
\Spin_{2n}\backslash\Spin_{2n+1}
or
\Sp_{2n}\times\Sp_{2n}\\
Sp_{4n}
. Both cases have
\check{\Phi}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}
of type
C
and
\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}^{\vee}\in\check{\mathfrak{X}}
lies in the
W_{\mathrm{X}}
-orbit of the unique minuscule fundamental weight of the dual group.
∎

We continue to impose Assumption
6.21
. Fix
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
as in the previous lemma. Since
({\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha},\mathrm{X}_{\alpha})
is
k_{\alpha}
-rational, the morphism
\check{\pi}_{\alpha,\mathrm{X}}
is only
\Gamma_{\alpha}
-equivariant with respect to the given action on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
and a unique
\Gamma_{\alpha}
-action on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}
. We now extend the
\Gamma_{\alpha}
-action on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}
to the representation
V_{\alpha}
of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}
. We may thus assume that
k=k_{\alpha}
and
{\mathrm{G}}={\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}
, thereby assuming that
{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
is absolutely simple. In particular,
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}=\{\alpha\}
.

By Lemma
9.6
,
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},der}
is simple of type
C
and acts on the standard representation, so that we must have
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},der}=\Sp_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
. In this case, the
\Gamma
-action on
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}
is trivial: since
\Sp_{2n}(\mathbb{C})
has trivial outer automorphism group, the
\ast
-action on
\Phi_{\mathrm{X}}
is trivial. In particular, the
\Gamma
-action on
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
preserves our fixed pinning
\{x_{\check{\gamma}}\}_{\check{\gamma}\in\check{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}}
induced by
\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
up to sign, so that the action is completely determined by a unique set of characters
\chi_{\gamma}:\Gamma_{\gamma}\longrightarrow\{\pm 1\}
such that
\sigma x_{\check{\gamma}}=\chi_{\gamma}(\sigma)x_{\check{\gamma}}
for
\sigma\in\Gamma_{\gamma}=\mathrm{Stab}_{\Gamma}(\check{\gamma})
and
\check{\gamma}\in\check{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Since pinnings of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
with respect to
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}
all lie in a single (free)
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}
-orbit, these characters combine to give a single character
\chi:\Gamma\to\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}
.

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
\chi:\Gamma\to\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}
denote the character uniquely determined by

\sigma x_{\check{\gamma}}=\chi_{\gamma}(\sigma)x_{\check{\gamma}}=\Ad(\chi(\sigma))x_{\check{\gamma}}

for all
\check{\gamma}\in\check{\Delta}^{sv}_{\mathrm{X}}
. For any quadratic character
\epsilon:\Gamma\longrightarrow\{\pm 1\}
, there exists a natural lift

\widetilde{\chi}_{\epsilon}\in\Hom_{cts}(\Gamma,\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}[2])\simeq H^{1}(\Gamma,\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}[2]).

Proof.

Consider the map
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}[2]\to\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}[2]
, which is not surjective. We first claim that for any choice of non-standard action,
\chi(\Gamma)
lies in the image of
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}[2]\to\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}[2]
. Considering the simple root
\check{\alpha}
of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
associated to
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
, we must have
\chi_{{\alpha}}\equiv 1
as
\alpha
is a root of type
A_{1}

[
KS17
, Lemma 10.4]
. That is, for any
\sigma\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
,
\Ad(\chi(\sigma))x_{\check{\alpha}}=x_{\check{\alpha}}.
This implies that the composition

\Gamma\overset{\chi}{\longrightarrow}\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}[2]\simeq X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad})/2X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad})\to X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad})/X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})\simeq\langle{\omega}_{\alpha}\rangle,

where
{\omega}_{\check{\alpha}}
is the fundamental coweight associated to
{\alpha}
, is trivial. This forces
\chi
to take value in

X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})/[X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})\cap 2X_{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad})],

implying our first claim.

We now construct a lift. Let
V_{\alpha}
denote the representation of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
from Lemma
9.6
, and let
l_{\B}\subset V
be the unique
\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}}
-stable line in
V_{\alpha}
. For any fixed quadratic character
\epsilon:\Gamma\longrightarrow\{\pm 1\}
, choose the unique lift

\widetilde{\chi}_{\epsilon}:\Gamma\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}[2]

of
\chi:\Gamma\to\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X},ad}[2]
satisfying that
\widetilde{\chi}_{\epsilon}(\sigma)v_{\B}=\epsilon(\sigma)v_{\B}
for any
v_{\B}\in l_{\B}
. This gives a lift.
∎

An important feature of this is that for any choice of
\epsilon
, the lift
\widetilde{\chi}_{\epsilon}
induces an action of
\Gamma
on
V
.

Continuing with the assumptions of the lemma, let
\check{\lambda}_{\alpha}\in X^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}})
be the dominant weight associated to
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
. We define
{\mathcal{D}}(\alpha)\subset{\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{X})
to be those colors satisfying
\rho(D)=\frac{1}{2}\check{\alpha}
. We define the
\Gamma
-representation

(9.2)

M(\alpha):=\mathbb{C}[{\mathcal{D}}(\alpha)]

where

(1)

if
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}
,
M(\alpha)
is determined by the
\Gamma
-action on the basis
{\mathcal{D}}(\alpha)
. In this case, we take
\epsilon\equiv 1
in the Lemma and use
\widetilde{\chi}_{1}
to give a
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\rtimes\Gamma
-action on
V_{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M(\alpha)
by letting
\sigma\in\Gamma
act by

\sigma(v\otimes m)=\widetilde{\chi}_{1}(\sigma)v\otimes\sigma(m).

In this case, the representation acts on
V_{\alpha}\otimes M(\alpha)\simeq V_{\alpha}\oplus V_{\alpha}
through

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\rtimes\Gamma\to\Sp(V_{\alpha})\times\mathrm{O}(M(\alpha))\subset\Sp(V_{\alpha}\otimes M(\alpha)).

(2)

If
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}\setminus{\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}}
, then
|{\mathcal{D}}(\alpha)|=1
so that
M(\alpha)=\mathbb{C}
. In this case, we take
\epsilon=\mu_{\alpha}
as in Lemma
6.22
, so that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\rtimes\Gamma
acts on
V_{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}M(\alpha)=V_{\alpha}
by letting
\sigma\in\Gamma
act by

\sigma(v)=\widetilde{\chi}_{\mu_{\alpha}}(\sigma)v.

This representation is clearly symplectic.

We now return to the general setting of
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
satisfying Assumption
6.21
and
\mathrm{H}
connected. For each
\Gamma
-orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
of distinguished roots and
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
, let
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}
be the induced group (cf.
[
Bor79
, 1.4,1.5]

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}=\Ind_{\Gamma/\Gamma_{\alpha}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}})\simeq\prod_{\Gamma/\Gamma_{\alpha}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}

such that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha,der}=[\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha},\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha}]=\Sp(V_{\alpha})
, where
V_{\alpha}=V(\check{\lambda}_{\alpha})
is the vector space of the associated representation. It follows from Lemma
9.6
that there exists a
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient map

(9.3)

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}.

For each
\Gamma
-orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
, we thus obtain a
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}\rtimes\Gamma
-representation

(9.4)

S_{\mathcal{O}}:=\Ind_{\Gamma_{\alpha}}^{\Gamma}(V_{\alpha}\otimes M(\alpha)).

More canonically, there exists a
\Gamma
-equivariant symplectic local system

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{O}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}=\Res_{k_{\alpha}/k}(\Spec(k_{\alpha})),

where
\Gamma_{\alpha}=\Gal(\overline{k}/k_{i})
and we have a
\Gamma
-representation
V_{\mathcal{O}}=H^{0}(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{O}})
(evidently, this construction is called
geometric induction
for an action groupoid
[
ABSA20
]
.) The group
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}
is then the automorphism group of
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{O}}
, which naturally acts on
V_{\mathcal{O}}
stalk-wise. This action extends to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}\rtimes\Gamma
.

To complete the proof of Theorem
9.4
, note that the
{}^{L}\mathrm{X}
-representation on

S_{\mathrm{X}}=\bigoplus_{\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}S_{\mathcal{O}}

obtained via pulling back along

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\rtimes\Gamma\overset{p\otimes\Delta}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{\mathcal{O}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}\rtimes\Gamma)

gives the sought-for extension.

9.1.1.
Application to rationality

The theorem below compares two
k
-forms
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
of a given symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}_{\overline{k}}
-variety
\overline{\mathrm{X}}
. For ease of notation, we say that
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
normally related to
(\mathrm{A},\B)
if the rational involutions
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
associated to
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
are both are normally related to
(\mathrm{A},\B)
. Since we may conjugate
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
by elements of
{\mathrm{G}}(k)
to ensure this, it leads to no loss in generality.

Theorem 9.8
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is quasi-split over
k
and suppose that
\overline{\mathrm{X}}=\overline{\mathrm{H}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\overline{k}}
is a symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}_{\overline{k}}
-variety satisfying Assumption
6.21
and that
\overline{\mathrm{H}}
is connected. Consider two
k
-rational
{\mathrm{G}}
-forms
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}=\mathrm{H}^{\prime}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
of
\overline{\mathrm{X}}
. Assume that
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
are both normally related to
(\mathrm{A},\B)
.

(1)

We have
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
.

(2)

Given a pair of distinguished morphisms
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
and
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
, there is a canonical isomorphism
f_{\mathrm{X}}:{}^{L}\mathrm{X}\simeq{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
inducing a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{{\mathrm{G}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}}

Suppose further there exists an
f_{\mathrm{X}}
-equivariant isomorphism
f_{S}:S_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
. Then
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
are
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner forms. If
H^{1}(k,\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\flat})=0
, then
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
are
k
-isomorphic.

Proof.

Let
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
be associated involutions for
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
,
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}={\mathrm{H}^{\prime}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
, respectively. Replacing
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
by
k
-rational conjugates if necessary, we may assume that both are normally related to
(\mathrm{A},\B)
. Since we have assumed that there is a
{\mathrm{G}}_{\overline{k}}
-equivariant isomorphism
\mathrm{X}_{\overline{k}}\simeq\mathrm{X}^{\prime}_{\overline{k}}
, this implies

\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}=\Delta_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}},\quad\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}=\hat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}},\>\text{ and }\>\theta|_{\mathrm{A}}=\theta^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{A}}.

Moreover,
\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
.

The construction of the associated group relies only on the inclusion of root system generated by the coroots of the associated roots of
\mathrm{X}
into the root system of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. Thus, we obtain a canonical identification
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
. Similarly, the construction of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
and the pinned
\Gamma
-action defined in terms of (
7.1
) are combinatorial and depend only on the
k
-group structure of
{\mathrm{G}}
, so that there exists
f_{\mathrm{X}}:{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}}\simeq{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
. The definition of
\vartheta
(resp.,
\vartheta^{\prime}
) is determined by
(\mathrm{A},\B)
and by the involution
\theta|_{\mathrm{A}}=\theta^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{A}}
(cf. Lemma
7.9
and Proposition
7.10
). By construction, we find that
\vartheta=\vartheta^{\prime}
so that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}^{\ast}
. Since distinguished morphisms have a finite kernel determined by the kernel of
\check{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{\mathrm{A}}
, we see that for any choice of such distinguished morphisms there exists a unique
f_{\mathrm{X}}
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{{\mathrm{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}}

commutes.

Assume now we are given an
f_{\mathrm{X}}
-equivariant isomorphism
f_{S}:S_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
. In particular, this restricts to an isomorphism of representations of algebraic groups (Proposition
9.4
)

(9.5)

\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}V(\check{\lambda}_{\alpha})\otimes M(\alpha)\simeq\bigoplus_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\check{\Delta}_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}^{dist}}V(\check{\lambda}_{\alpha^{\prime}})\otimes M(\alpha^{\prime}),

which induces a bijection
\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Delta_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}^{dist}
by highest weight theory; this is uniquely determined by the isomorphism
f_{\mathrm{X}}
. This is
\Gamma
-equivariant and we obtain an identification of Galois orbits
\Gamma\cdot\gamma_{i}\mapsto\Gamma\cdot\gamma^{\prime}_{i}
. Recall that there is an isomorphism of
\Gamma
-modules

S^{(\check{\B}_{\mathrm{X}})}_{\mathrm{X}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\mathcal{O}_{i}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}\Ind_{\Gamma_{i}}^{\Gamma}(M(\alpha_{i})).

To recover the character
\mu_{i}
, not that the
\Gamma_{i}
-action on
M(\alpha_{i})
is uniquely determined by this character and there is a canonical
\Gamma_{i}
-equivariant morphism

\displaystyle\Ind_{\Gamma_{i}}^{\Gamma}(M(\alpha_{i}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M(\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle[f:\Gamma\to M(\alpha_{i})]

\displaystyle\longmapsto f(1),

from which we may compute
\mu_{i}
. Now passing to highest weight spaces in (
9.5
), we obtain a
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}
-equivariant isomorphism
M(\alpha_{i})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}M(\alpha_{i}^{\prime})
. In particular,
\mu_{i}=\mu_{i}^{\prime}
.

All together, we see that
\out_{\mathrm{X}}=\out_{\mathrm{X}^{\prime}}
, which implies that
\mathrm{X}
and
\mathrm{X}^{\prime}
are inner forms. The final claim is now a direct consequence of Lemma
2.7
.∎

Remark 9.9
.

In general, one can only recover
\mathrm{X}
up to
{\mathrm{G}}
-inner twist from the data of the dual group and
S_{\mathrm{X}}
(c.f Example
8.12
). On the other hand, we do obtain uniqueness in the following cases:

(1)

If
\mathrm{X}
is automorphism free, then the
k
-rational form
\mathrm{X}
is uniquely determined up to isomorphism by the data of
{}^{L}\mathrm{X}\to{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}}
and
S_{\mathrm{X}}
.

(2)

More generally, Lemma
3.22
implies that if
\mathrm{H}
is spherically closed (equivalently,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\sharp}=\{1\}
), then
\mathrm{X}
uniquely determined by the data of
{}^{L}\mathrm{X}\to{}^{L}{\mathrm{G}}
and
S_{\mathrm{X}}
, up to isomorphism.

(3)

The weaker property that
Z({\mathrm{G}})\subset\mathrm{H}
also suffices, so that uniqueness holds for all inner symmetric spaces (including all hermitian symmetric varieties).

9.2.
Relation to endoscopy

We now reinterpret the notion of endoscopic data in terms of the symplectic representation
S_{\mathrm{X}}
. For simplicity, we will now impose the following conditions:

(1)

{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
is simply connected,

(2)

\mathrm{X}
is
excellent
(cf. Definition
7.4
).

We note that this forces
\mathrm{X}
to have no spherical roots of type
N
and that
\mathrm{H}
is geometrically connected (cf. Lemma
B.4
). In particular,
\mathrm{X}
satisfies Assumption
6.21
and any distinguished morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{X}}
is injective.

Recall that Lemma
9.6
gives a
\Gamma
-equivariant quotient map

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\overset{p}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}};

here, for each orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
, there is a unique isomorphism such that

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}=\Ind_{\Gamma/\Gamma_{\alpha}}(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}})\simeq\prod_{\Gamma/\Gamma_{\alpha}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}

such that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha,der}=[\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha},\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\alpha}]=\Sp(V_{\alpha})
.

Suppose now that
\mathfrak{e}=({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\theta_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\kappa,{}^{L}\eta)
is an extended quasi-split endoscopic datum (cf. Section
8.3.1
). Clearly
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is also excellent since the kernel of
\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
is a
k
-form of the kernel of
\mathrm{A}\to{\mathrm{A}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. Thus,
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
possesses no roots of type
N
, is well adapted, and Conjecture
3.17
holds for
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. In particular, we obtain a
{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
-representation
S_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
from Proposition
9.4
.

Under the identification
\eta(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}})=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\kappa}
, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{O}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{O}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Here, the lower left-hand entry is the image
p(\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\kappa})
; in particular, for each
\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
, we obtain a subgroup
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}\subset\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}.
For each
\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
, recall from (
9.4
) that we have the
\check{G}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}\rtimes\Gamma
-representation
S_{\mathcal{O}}
. When
\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}
, this representation decomposes into two irreducible factors

S_{\mathcal{O}}=S_{\mathcal{O},1}\oplus S_{\mathcal{O},\mu_{i}}

according to the
\Gamma_{i}
-equivariant decomposition
M(\alpha_{i})=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}(\mu_{i})
; when
\mathcal{O}\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}\setminus{\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{(2)}}
,
S_{\mathcal{O}}
is irreducible.

Lemma 9.10
.

The restriction of each irreducible factor of
S_{\mathcal{O}}
to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}\rtimes\Gamma
remains irreducible.

Proof.

In fact, since each factor restricts to the irreducible
\check{G}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}
-representation
S_{\mathcal{O}}=\prod_{\alpha\in\mathcal{O}}S_{\alpha}
, it suffices to show that it remains irreducible upon restriction to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}
; that is, we may ignore the
\Gamma
-action. In this geometric setting, each irreducible factor of
S_{\mathcal{O}}
is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathcal{O}}V_{\alpha}
.

Moreover, the representation restricts irreducibly to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}
if and only if it restricts irreducibly to
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}\cap\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},der}
. We thus assume that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},der}=\prod_{\alpha}\Sp(V_{\alpha})
and consider the restriction of
S_{\mathcal{O}}
to

\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}\cap\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},der}=:\prod_{\alpha}\Sp(V_{\alpha})_{\kappa_{\alpha}},

where
\Sp(V_{\alpha})_{\kappa_{\alpha}}\subset\Sp(V_{\alpha})
denotes the corresponding subgroup.

We claim that for each
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
, the subgroup
\Sp(V_{\alpha})_{\kappa_{\alpha}}\subset\Sp(V_{\alpha})
is the stabilizer of a semi-simple element
\kappa_{\alpha}
of a symmetric variety of the form
\Sp(V_{\alpha})\backslash\GL(V_{\alpha})
, up to central torus. Indeed, for any
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
the projection
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\alpha}
from Lemma
9.6
induces a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\alpha},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{s}_{\alpha}}

where the notations on the bottom row have the obvious meaning. This implies that if
x=\eta(\hat{s}_{\mathfrak{e}}(\kappa))\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}
, then
\kappa_{\alpha}\in\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\alpha}
and up to center,
\Sp(V_{\alpha})_{\kappa_{\alpha}}
is the stabilizer of
\kappa_{\alpha}
. Appealing to Lemma
6.20
, the symmetric space
\hat{\mathrm{X}}_{\alpha,der}
of the form
\Sp(V_{\alpha})\backslash\SL(V_{\alpha})
9
9

9

This relies on Assumption
6.21
, since
(\Sp_{2n},\GL_{n})
gives a counterexample.
. A simple calculation implies that
\Sp(V_{\alpha})_{\kappa_{\alpha}}
respects the orthogonal decomposition

V_{\alpha}=\bigoplus_{a}V_{a},

where
\{a\}\subset\mathbb{C}
are the roots of the minimal polynomial of
\kappa_{\alpha}
, and
V_{a}
is the corresponding eigenspace, so that
\kappa_{\alpha}|_{V_{i}}=aI_{V_{a}}
. Then
\Sp(V_{\alpha})_{\kappa_{\alpha}}=\prod_{a}\Sp(V_{a})
, and the restriction is irreducible.
∎

In particular, the dual group
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
possesses two symplectic representations
S_{\mathrm{X}}
and
S_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
via the two quotients

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{\mathcal{U}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{dist}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathcal{U}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathfrak{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Verifying the existence of the dotted arrow is now an exercise in highest weight theory. It is compatible with the corresponding map

\prod_{\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2})\simeq\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})\overset{\mathrm{dist}_{\mathfrak{e}}}{\longrightarrow}{\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})}\simeq\prod_{\mathcal{U}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{dist}}\Res_{k_{i}/k}(\mu_{2})

constructed in the proof of Theorem
8.5
. Note that
\mathrm{dist}_{\mathfrak{e}}
need not be surjective.

Since
S_{\mathrm{X}}
is the standard minuscule representation of the derived subgroup of
\prod_{\mathcal{O}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X},\mathcal{O},\kappa}
, and likewise for
\prod_{\mathcal{U}\subset\Delta_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}^{dist}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathcal{U}}
, the existence of the dashed morphism of algebraic groups implies the existence of a a
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
-linear injective homomorphism

\rho_{\mathfrak{e}}:S_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}.

Corollary 9.11
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is an excellent symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety and
\mathfrak{e}
is a quasi-split endoscopic datum associate to the
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
-variety
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}=\mathrm{H}_{\mathfrak{e}}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. There is a
{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
-linear injection of representations

\rho_{\mathfrak{e}}:S_{\mathrm{X}^{\mathfrak{e}}}|_{{}^{L}{\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}}}\longrightarrow S_{\mathrm{X}}.

Example 9.12
.

Note that the map need not be surjective. For example, if
{\mathrm{G}}=\GL_{2n+1}
and
\mathrm{X}=\GL_{2n+1}/\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n+1}
, then
\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X})=\{1\}
. This variety has

\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{X}}=\mathrm{A},\qquad\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\GL_{2n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\text{ and }\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\GL_{2n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}/[\Sp_{2n}\times\{1\}].

An easy exercise shows that for any
a+b=n
with
a,b>0
,
{\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}}=\GL_{2a}\times\GL_{2b+1}
is an endoscopic group with endoscopic variety
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}=\GL_{2a}/[\GL_{a}\times\GL_{a}]\times\GL_{2b+1}/[\GL_{b}\times\GL_{b+1}]
. Here
\Aut_{d}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})\simeq\mu_{2}
.

9.3.
The dual Hamiltonian variety and Lagrangian correspondences

We may now remark on the relationship between the results of the previous section on recent work and conjectures of Ben-Zvi, Sakellaridis, and Venkatesh
[
BZSV23
]
.

We continue to assume that
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is excellent, so we may regard
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
as a subgroup of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. Let
\rho_{\mathrm{X}}:\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})\longrightarrow\check{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{X}}
be the principal
\SL_{2}(\mathbb{C})
morphism determined by a fixed pinning of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. This gives rise to an
\mathfrak{sl}(2)
-triple
(e,h,f)
in
\check{\mathfrak{l}}_{\mathrm{X}}

e=d\rho_{\mathrm{X}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad h=d\rho_{\mathrm{X}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\quad f=d\rho_{\mathrm{X}}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right).

The element
h
induces a grading
\check{\mathfrak{g}}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\check{g}_{i}
; let
\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}
to be the unipotent group whose Lie algebra is
\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}:=\bigoplus_{i\geq 1}\check{g}_{i}
. By construction, the nilpotent orbit
\mathcal{O}_{e}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}\cdot e
is induced from the principal orbit of the Lie subalgebra
\check{\mathfrak{l}}_{\mathrm{X}}
. It may happen that
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}\neq 0
; set
\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{i\geq 2}\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}
and let
\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}^{+}
denote the associated unipotent group. Using the Killing form
\kappa(-,-)
, we obtain a character
\psi_{\mathrm{X}}:\check{\U}^{+}_{\mathrm{X}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}
via

\psi_{\mathrm{X}}(\exp(u))=\kappa(f,u);

this gives an element in
\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
. Let
\check{\mathrm{R}}_{\mathrm{X}}=Z_{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}(e,f,h)
denote the centralizer of the
\mathfrak{sl}(2)
-triple. It is well known that the vector space
\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}/\check{\mathfrak{u}}^{+}_{\mathrm{X}}
carries a
\check{\mathrm{R}}_{\mathrm{X}}
-invariant symplectic form

\kappa(v,w):=\kappa(f,[v,w]).

Noting that
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset\check{\mathrm{R}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, we may thus view this as a Hamiltonian
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}
-space, where
\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}
acts additively via the isomorphism
\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}/\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}^{+}\simeq\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}/\check{\mathfrak{u}}^{+}_{\mathrm{X}}
and the moment map

\mu_{\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}}:\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}/\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}^{+}\longrightarrow\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}

is shifted by
\iota_{\mathrm{X}}
; that is
\mu_{\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}}(u)=\kappa(f,[u,-])+\iota_{\mathrm{X}}
. Finally,
[
BZSV23
, Section 4.3]
defines a representation
S_{\mathrm{X}}
of
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, which they conjecture to be symplectic, giving rise to a moment map

\mu_{\mathrm{X}}:S_{\mathrm{X}}\to\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}.

With this preparation, Ben-Zvi, Sakellaridis, and Venkatesh consider the Hamiltonian
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
-space

\check{\mathcal{M}}:=[S_{\mathrm{X}}\times(\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}/\check{\mathfrak{u}}^{+}_{\mathrm{X}})]\times_{(\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathrm{X}}+\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\ast}}^{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}}T^{\ast}\check{{\mathrm{G}}},

equipped with a moment map
\mu:\check{\mathcal{M}}\to\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ast}
induced by the moment map of
T^{\ast}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. More precisely, note that we also have

\check{\mathcal{M}}\simeq[S_{\mathrm{X}}\times(\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}/\check{\mathfrak{u}}^{+}_{\mathrm{X}})\times_{(\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathrm{X}}+\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}})^{\ast}}\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ast}]\times^{\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\check{U}_{\mathrm{X}}}\check{{\mathrm{G}}},

since
T^{\ast}\check{{\mathrm{G}}}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ast}\times\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
. Then

\mu(([v,u],X),g)=X.

Now suppose that
\mathrm{X}
is an excellent symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety. We claim that the symplectic representation
S_{\mathrm{X}}
constructed in Section
9.1
gives precisely the representation conjectured in
[
BZSV23
]
. In fact, by inspection of Section 4.1 of
[
BZSV23
]
, their definition of
S_{\mathrm{X}}
is intimately related to the notion of distinguished spherical roots of
[
Los09
]
. Indeed, recall the notation
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{X})^{dist}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{X})
from Section
9.1
, giving the subset of colors
D
of
\mathrm{X}
such that
\rho(D)
is dual to
2\alpha
for some
\alpha\in\Delta_{\mathrm{X}}^{dist}
. These are precisely the
colors of even sphere type

[
BZSV23
, Section 4.3]
; see
[
BZSV23
, Definition 4.3.4.]
for the motivation for this name.

In particular, our construction of
S_{\mathrm{X}}
, motivated entirely by rationality concerns, recovers their definition exactly. Moreover, the content of Section
9.1
proves the following special case of their conjectures. We leave the details to the interested reader; they essentially follow from Proposition
9.4
.

Proposition 9.13
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{G}}
is quasi-split over
k
and that
\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
is an excellent symmetric
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety. Then
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{X}}^{max}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{X}}
and Conjecture 4.3.16 of
[
BZSV23
]
holds for
\mathrm{X}
.

The previous sections show that
S_{\mathrm{X}}
comes equipped with a natural
\Gamma
-action determined by the geometric class of
\mathrm{X}
as a
{\mathrm{G}}
-variety over
k
. This action is compatible with the
\Gamma
-actions on
T^{\ast}{\mathrm{G}}
,
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, and
\check{\mathfrak{u}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, and therefore induces a
\Gamma
-action on
\check{\mathcal{M}}
.
We note that both the moment map
\mu
and the bundle map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathrm{X}}:=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\check{{\mathrm{G}}}}

are
\Gamma
-equivariant with respect to this
\Gamma
-action on
\check{\mathcal{M}}
.

For simplicity, let us now assume that
{\mathrm{G}}_{der}
is absolutely simple. In Section
7.2
, we considered the
symmetric variety

\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, where
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is the associated group of Knop–Schalke. One can verify that
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset\check{\mathrm{R}}_{\mathrm{X}}
, with equality holding if
\mathrm{X}_{sc}
is not a
k
-form of
\SL_{2n+1}/S(\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n+1})
(see also
[
Nad05
, Section 10.7]
). In particular,
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\check{\mathrm{R}}_{\mathrm{X}}
is always spherical. Moreover,
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\cap\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}=\{1\}
, so that there is a distinguished closed embedding

\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\subset\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}\check{\U}_{\mathrm{X}}\backslash\check{{\mathrm{G}}}=\check{\mathrm{X}}.

When
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
, we have
\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\check{\mathrm{X}}
.

Suppose now that
\mathfrak{e}
is an endoscopic datum for
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
associated to
({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})
; the discussion in Section
9.2
shows that
\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}}
also does not have type
N
roots, so that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{e}}=T^{\ast}(\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})
is hyperspherical. Based on the expectation that relative functoriality is mediated by the properties of the dual Hamiltonian variety, it is natural to expect a symplectic map
\check{\mathcal{M}}\to\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\ast};}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\mathfrak{e}}}

commutes. This is true in the “the category of Hamiltonian spaces
and Lagrangian correspondences,” compatibly with the expectations of
[
BZSV23
, Remark 3.1.3]
.

Proposition 9.14
.

Suppose that
({\mathrm{G}},\mathrm{X})
are as above and assume that
\mathrm{X}
is a quasi-split symmetric variety.
For an endoscopic datum
\mathfrak{e}
, suppose that
\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
is the Hamiltonian
\check{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
-variety dual to
({\mathrm{G}}_{\mathfrak{e}},\mathrm{X}_{\mathfrak{e}})
. There is a Lagrangian correspondence of Hamiltonian varieties

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{\mathcal{M}}.}

covering the map
\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ast}\to\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\ast}_{\mathfrak{e}}
.

We expect there is an analogue of this statement in the non-quasi-split setting which respects the Whittaker reduction to
\check{\mathcal{M}}_{slice}
in
[
BZSV23
, Section 14.3]
, but have not checked this.

Proof.

The only symmetric varieties satisfying that
\mathrm{X}
is excellent and that
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
are Galois symmetric pairs and forms of
\GL_{2n}/\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n}
(the type
A_{2n-1}
case). Dropping the assumption
\hat{{\mathrm{G}}}_{\mathrm{X}}=\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
includes forms of
\GL_{2n+1}/\GL_{n}\times\GL_{n+1}
(the type
A_{2n}
case). In all cases, we have that
\mathcal{M}\simeq T^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast})
where

\check{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}=\begin{cases}\quad\check{\mathrm{X}}&:\mathrm{X}\text{ is Galois or type $A_{2n}$,}\\
\check{\mathrm{X}}\times\mathbb{A}^{2n}&:\mathrm{X}\text{ is type $A_{2n-1}$.}\end{cases}

Moreover, for an endoscopic datum
\mathfrak{e}
, there is an induced embedding
\check{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\ast}\subset\check{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}
; we note that these embeddings
do not respect the gradings of these varieties
. In particular, we take the fiber product

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{e}}:=\check{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\ast}\times_{\check{\mathrm{X}}^{\ast}}\check{\mathcal{M}}.

Now Corollary
9.11
implies that there exists a symplectic map
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{e}}\to\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
. Since
\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}
always has a similar polarization
\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}=T^{\ast}(\check{\mathrm{X}}_{\mathfrak{e}}^{\ast})
, it is easy to check that the induced map
\mathcal{L}\to\check{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathfrak{e}}\times\check{\mathcal{M}}
is Lagrangian.
∎

10.
Examples and applications

In this final section, we give examples of endoscopic symmetric varieties as well as some applications. We first discuss an example related to unitary periods. In Section
10.2
, we establish the prestabilization of the regular elliptic part of the relative trace formula for a symmetric variety, under certain simplifying assumptions to streamline the presentation, deferring the technical general statement to
[
Les24
]
. The key point is Proposition
10.5
. We end with a table of examples in Section
10.3
.

10.1.
Example with unitary groups

As noted in the introduction, we expect that endoscopy for the relative Langlands program will involve, in its global applications, cases where periods of automorphic forms are given by sums of Euler products. We discuss this in a context that arises naturally in the theory of endoscopy for the symmetric variety
\mathrm{X}=\U_{n}\times\U_{n}\backslash\U_{2n}

[
Les19a
,
Les22
]
.

Among other steps, the proof of the endoscopic fundamental lemma in this case involves reducing the matching of orbital integrals on
\mathrm{X}_{n}:=\U_{n}\times\U_{n}\backslash\U_{2n}
and its endoscopic varieties
\mathrm{X}_{a}\times\mathrm{X}_{b}
with
a+b=n
to a matching of
\kappa
-orbital integrals on
{\mathrm{Y}}_{n}=\U_{n}\backslash\Res_{E/k}(\GL_{n})
to stable orbital integrals on
{\mathrm{Y}}_{a}\times{\mathrm{Y}}_{b}
for
a+b=n
, for entire modules of the relevant spherical Hecke algebras; we refer the interested reader to
[
Les19a
, Section 2]
. Such a matching of orbital integrals plays the role of the fundamental lemma for the stabilization of the relative trace formula of
\U_{n}
-periods on
\Res_{E/k}(\GL_{n})
. On the other hand, a great deal of information is known about these period integrals in the literature. The goal of this section is to discuss this comparison in terms of the numerical conjectures of
[
BZSV23
, Section 14]
and the known evaluation of unitary periods for Eisenstein series due to Lapid–Rogawski for
n=3

[
LR03
]
and Feigen–Lapid–Offen in general
[
FLO12
]
.

With the discussion in Section
9.3
, the dual Hamiltonian variety for
\mathcal{M}=T^{\ast}{\mathrm{Y}}_{n}
is the
{}^{L}\Res_{E/k}(\GL_{n})
-variety
\check{\mathcal{M}}=T^{\ast}(\GL_{n}\backslash(\GL_{n})^{2})
; here the Galois action factors through
\Gamma_{E/k}=\Gal(E/k)
and acts via swapping the two factors of
\GL_{n}^{2}
fixing the diagonally embedded
\GL_{n}
point-wise. Set
{\mathrm{G}}=\Res_{E/k}(\GL_{n})
and
\mathrm{H}=\U_{n}
, so that
{\mathrm{Y}}_{n}=\mathrm{H}\backslash{\mathrm{G}}
. Noting that the endoscopic varieties
{\mathrm{Y}}_{a}\times{\mathrm{Y}}_{b}
are associated to Levi subgroups, one anticipates that endoscopic properties of
{\mathrm{Y}}_{n}
only involve induced representations. Here,
\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\check{\mathrm{X}}=\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C})\backslash(\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C}))^{2}\simeq\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C})
, on which
\Gamma_{E/k}
acts via inversion. In particular,

\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\heartsuit,\Gamma}=\hat{\mathrm{X}}^{\Gamma}=\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C})[2]

is just the
2
-torsion, so that
{\mathrm{Y}}_{a}\times{\mathrm{Y}}_{b}
for
a+b=n
is a complete list representatives of the equivalence classes of endoscopic varieties.

We now consider the conjectural recipe for the global period problem: suppose for the sake of simplicity that to an automorphic representation of
{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{A})
we may associate a global parameter
\phi:L_{k}\longrightarrow\check{{\mathrm{G}}}
; this is clearly conjectural, but works everywhere locally. In any case, we are merely interpreting a conjecture of
[
BZSV23
]
in terms of the notion of endoscopy. In this setting, the global conjecture of
[
SV17
]
states that an automorphic representation
\pi
of
{\mathrm{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{k})=\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}_{E})
with Arthur parameter
\phi:L_{k}\to{}^{L}\Res_{E/k}(\GL_{n})
is
\mathrm{H}
-distinguished if and only if
\phi
factors through

{}^{L}{\mathrm{Y}}_{n}=\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\Gamma\longrightarrow{}^{L}\Res_{E/k}(\GL_{n}).

Generalizing slightly the conjectures of
[
BZSV23
, Section 14]
, one anticipates that the
\mathrm{H}
-period of an automorphic form
\varphi\in\pi
is related to the sum of
L
-values

(10.1)

\sum_{x}\frac{L(\phi,T_{x}^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})}{L(\phi,\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{x})},

where
x
runs over
\phi
-fixed points of
\hat{\mathrm{X}}=\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C})\backslash(\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C}))^{2}
,
T_{x}^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})
is the cotangent space of
x
, and
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{x}
is the Lie algebra of the stabilizer of
x
. The precise
L
-values
L(\phi,V)
are defined in
[
BZSV23
, Section 14.1]
.

By the preceding statement, we may assume that the base point
x=x_{0}
is fixed by
\phi
; this records that
\pi
arises as a base change from
\GL_{n}
. In this case, the stabilizer is
\GL_{n}(\mathbb{C})
,
\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{x}\simeq\mathfrak{gl}_{n}
, and
T_{x}^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})=\mathfrak{gl}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}
, where
\mathbb{C}_{\omega}
is the one dimensional
\Gamma
-representation where
\Gamma
acts through the quadratic character
\omega:=\omega_{E/k}
associated to the extension
E/k
by local class field theory. The same calculation holds for the central point
(I_{n},-I_{n})\in\check{\mathrm{X}}
, producing the same period. This is compatible with
[
FLO12
, Theorem 10.2]
expressing the unitary period of a cuspidal automorphic form as an Euler product times
2L(1,\pi\times\check{\pi}\cdot\omega)
, where
\check{\pi}
is the contragredient. In particular, we see the factor of
2
in the period calculation as relating to the two fixed points
(I_{n},\pm I_{n})
.

Now if we assume that
\phi=\phi_{a}\times\phi_{b}
also factors through the Levi subgroup
\GL_{a}\times\GL_{b}\subset\GL_{n}
(so corresponding to an Eisenstein series), then the four additional points

\{(\pm I_{a},\mp I_{b}),\pm(\pm I_{a},\mp I_{b})\}

are fixed by
\phi
. In these cases

T_{x}^{\ast}(\hat{\mathrm{X}})=[\mathfrak{gl}_{a}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}]\oplus[\mathfrak{gl}_{b}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}]\oplus[\mathrm{Std}_{a}\otimes\mathrm{Std}_{b}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}]\oplus[\mathrm{Std}^{\vee}_{a}\otimes\mathrm{Std}_{b}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}],

while

\check{\mathfrak{g}}_{x}=[\mathfrak{gl}_{a}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{b}]\oplus[\mathrm{Std}_{a}\otimes\mathrm{Std}_{b}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}]\oplus[\mathrm{Std}^{\vee}_{a}\otimes\mathrm{Std}_{b}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\omega}];

In particular, the corresponding quotient term (
10.1
) gives
